# Nightjar development notes

Detailed implementation and local QA notes accumulated during development. Earlier validation counts and limitations may be superseded by later entries. For current scope and acceptance, see [BETA-HANDOFF.md](./BETA-HANDOFF.md) and [RELEASE-CHECKLIST.md](./RELEASE-CHECKLIST.md).

## Cloudflare deployment from GitHub

See [CLOUDFLARE.md](./CLOUDFLARE.md) for Workers Builds settings. Use `npm run verify` as the Workers Builds build command to check types, lint, tests, production output and packaging before `npm run deploy`. To check packaging separately without publishing, use `npm run deploy:check`.

Optional red-light display mode tints the page, including charts and Moon imagery, and remembers the setting in browser local storage. It does not control screen brightness or browser/OS surfaces; colour-coded charts change appearance while enabled.

Sky atlas saved targets: save/remove catalogue objects locally, with current altitude and horizon status. The list persists across reloads and remains visible independently of search/horizon filters.

Saved sky targets can be downloaded as a plain-text observing checklist with UTC and local time, coordinates, altitude, azimuth, horizon status and space for notes. The list is a snapshot of the selected observing time and place.

Each sky target supports up to 2,000 characters of personal notes, automatically saved on the current browser and included when exporting favourites. Removing a favourite retains its notes; clearing the notes field removes that note. Notes do not sync between devices or hosting domains.

The full sky atlas offers independent star-pattern line and object-label display controls. Hiding labels retains the selected target label and cardinal directions. The small bright-star chart remains an approximate planning view.

Visible stars, planets and the Moon can be selected directly from the full sky chart by pointer or keyboard. Selection highlights the object and links back to its details; the small dashboard preview remains a static overview. Overlapping tap areas resolve to the nearest plotted object, and pointer selection focuses that object for subsequent keyboard actions.

Expand sky atlas opens an accessible full-screen viewer on phones and a larger dialog on desktop. Zoom controls run from 100% to 300%; the chart has its own native scroll area for touch, trackpad and keyboard navigation. Opening the viewer resets zoom, while target selection remains shared with the main atlas. View target details closes the viewer and focuses the selected object’s details; Escape or Close returns focus to the expand control. The viewer preserves the chosen chart layers, observing site and time.

The Sky atlas also has a separate Milky Way planner. Its optional dashed marker shows the direction of the galactic centre when above the horizon, using NASA's published position. The card reports altitude and azimuth at the chosen time, then samples the next 24 hours in 15-minute steps for fully dark periods with the direction at least 10° up. It shows the best contiguous dark window, the highest sampled point, Moon status, and a shortcut to that observing time. This is a sightline for planning, not a visible point source or a full Milky Way map; local obstructions, clouds and light pollution are not modelled.

The meteor calendar sorts the next annual occurrences after the selected observing time. For each typical local peak date pair, it samples hourly astronomical darkness and Moon-below-horizon conditions, identifies the longest contiguous Moon-free window, and shows an average cloud forecast when that date is covered by the seven-day weather data. Shower rates remain ideal reference values; radiant height and the exact annual peak are not calculated. Calendar downloads retain their typical overnight all-day window.

The Tonight dashboard groups future hourly weather into local observing nights (noon to noon) and shows up to seven nights with astronomical darkness. Each card scores the best consecutive dark forecast-hour pair using the existing cloud/darkness/Moon heuristic, displays forecast coverage, and sets the shared observing time when selected. At an incomplete forecast edge it can use one hour. A card can also download a tentative timed calendar event with the site, forecast score and cloud/Moon context; calendar apps display its UTC event times in their own time zone. This is a planning comparison, not a seeing or transparency prediction.

The 3D Moon renderer is isolated from lunar facts and eclipses and uses static Three.js imports so unused exports can be removed from the production bundle. Partial initialization and texture failures dispose allocated WebGL resources. A failed globe download has its own recovery boundary.

When an updated service worker is waiting, Nightjar offers Update and reload. Activation requires that action; automatic first installation does not reload the planner. The selected observing time is saved for one-use recovery, while saved places, targets, notes and equipment stay in local storage. Real-device installation/update behavior still needs iPhone and Android validation.

Live forecast refresh: weather and site comparisons refresh every 15 minutes while visible and online; aurora refreshes every five minutes. Returning to a stale tab or reconnecting triggers a refresh, with duplicate lifecycle events throttled. Refreshing never changes the chosen observing time. Tonight retains its last successful forecast for the same coordinates if a refresh fails, labels that fallback, and warns once it was fetched over 30 minutes ago. The displayed retrieval time is not the weather model's issue time. Requests revalidate browser caches and have a 20-second client timeout; manual refresh remains available after a failure. No forecast is persisted for offline use.

Observing time entry supports UTC or the observing site's local time zone. The local date picker uses the site's zone rather than the device's zone, including non-hour offsets. Missing daylight-saving hours are rejected without changing the selected sky. Repeated hours require an explicit choice between the two UTC offsets. Switching entry modes preserves the selected instant; UTC remains the initial entry mode. Pending invalid or ambiguous entries do not change forecasts, exports or saved observing time.

Manual plan backup is available in My places. Download a versioned JSON file containing saved sites (including Bortle ratings), favourite targets, target notes and imaging equipment. Import reads a file locally and previews its contents before any write. Merge preserves existing sites within 0.0001° in each coordinate, equipment with matching trimmed names, and notes for the same target; favourites are combined. It preserves the current site and observing instant and refreshes the affected UI without reloading. It does not perform cloud sync or include live forecasts. Backups are limited to 1 MB, 100 saved sites, 39 targets, 100 notes of up to 2,000 characters, and 100 equipment profiles. Invalid files and unsupported versions are rejected. Storage write failures attempt to roll back completed writes and explicitly report if rollback fails. Keep a copy of the file before moving to another device or domain.

Run `npm run test:backup` for backup validation, merge, round-trip and storage rollback regression checks.

The import review shows the file's UTC export date, additions by category, resulting totals and the names of differing sites, notes and equipment whose existing versions will be kept. Identical records are not listed as conflicts. A regression test checks this distinction against the merge result. Desktop and mobile browser checks use a synthetic file to verify the additions, a conflicting Vega note, readable controls and cancellation without importing any data.

When a backup is ready to review, keyboard focus moves to its heading and scrolls it into view with a top margin. Cancelling returns focus to the file picker. Desktop and mobile browser checks verify both focus transitions; no data is written by previewing or cancelling.

Offline recovery retains the selected UTC instant when Try again online is used. The offline page preserves red-light display mode, shows connection hints without automatically navigating away, retains note line breaks and up to 2,000 characters, and warns when storage cannot be read. Numeric saved fields are validated without coercing missing coordinates to zero. Its updated offline document uses the v4 service-worker cache, so existing installations receive it through the normal update prompt. Run `npm run test:offline` for standalone offline-page regression checks.

Sky atlas also offers Download print-ready plan for saved targets. The self-contained HTML file includes interactive checkboxes, full notes and blank note space, exact UTC and site-local time with UTC offset, site coordinates/Bortle rating, snapshot Sun/Moon conditions and validated saved equipment. It opens without external resources and offers the browser's Print or save as PDF action. Tick marks persist only while the file stays open. Print styles remove the controls and use a paper layout; live weather is not included. Names and notes are escaped as plain text. The HTML checklist does not replace the JSON data backup.

Run `npm test` for all repository regression tests, or `npm run test:plan` for standalone observing-plan export checks.


The full Sky atlas now opens in an interactive 3D horizon dome, with the 2D overhead chart available beside it. Both views use the same target positions, time, site, selected object, pattern lines, label setting and galactic-core direction. Orientation guidance and the expand control follow the active view. A photo-and-details shortcut above either chart scrolls to and focuses the selected target details. The dome can be rotated, tilted, zoomed and expanded using pointer, touch gestures or keyboard controls. A Centre selected object control brings an above-horizon target into the middle of the view, including when ordinary labels are hidden. Selected-target status shows altitude and azimuth; centring is disabled below the horizon. Reset view restores the whole-dome camera. It renders on demand and releases its WebGL resources on unmount. If WebGL is unavailable or its context is lost, the 2D chart is shown. The dome is a schematic direction model: object sizes and distances are not represented to scale.

Selected target details include reference photos with credits and source links. Moon/planet images come from NASA/JPL spacecraft archives; enhanced or false colour and mosaics are labelled. Bright stars use 15 × 15 arcminute archival DSS red-band photographic fields centred on catalogue coordinates, not resolved stellar surfaces. DSS images are provided by STScI with survey copyrights and acknowledgements linked. Images load only for the selected object, require a connection, and have an explicit retry state on failure. They do not follow the selected time or Moon phase.

Reference photos can be opened in an accessible enlarged viewer with 100–300% zoom, native scrolling, Escape-to-close and focus restoration. Opening it again resets zoom. Captions, source links and archival-image limitations remain available in portrait and landscape layouts. Enlargement uses the original reference URL and does not increase image resolution.

The expanded 3D atlas includes the same object-label, star-pattern and core-direction display controls as the main chart. These three settings are saved independently on this browser, shared with the main chart and applied when switching back to 2D. They restore after reloads; missing or invalid fields default to shown. Failed writes leave the controls usable for the current visit and show a status message. Tests cover validation, storage failures and isolation from other saved planner data. Browser checks cover changes from the expanded 3D viewer, reload restoration, mobile 2D restoration and the selected target remaining labelled with ordinary labels hidden.

The expanded 2D chart also includes these shared display controls. Short landscape layouts keep display/zoom tools in a scrollable sidebar alongside the chart; short narrow windows scroll the dialog with a sticky close header. Browser checks cover all three toggles, selected labels, shared main-chart state, reopening at 100% zoom, portrait/landscape/short layouts, and focus return to either the expand button or target details.

The chosen 3D or 2D atlas view is saved on this browser and restored after section changes and page reloads. Restoring 2D avoids mounting the 3D renderer. Missing or invalid preferences default to 3D. If browser storage is unavailable, view switching still works for the current visit and a status message explains that the choice could not be saved. Regression tests cover preference validation and storage failures; browser checks cover both modes across reloads and the mobile layout. Installed-PWA restart testing remains outstanding.

The When to look calculations live in `lib/visibility-window.ts`. Regression coverage checks strict altitude above 30°, descending peak ranking, daylight versus after-civil-twilight opportunities, a London astronomical fixture, an empty polar window, and distinct UTC samples through the repeated UK local hour.

On short, narrow windows, the 3D atlas and enlarged-photo dialogs scroll while their headers stay visible, keeping the close buttons reachable. Browser checks cover shared expanded display settings, selected labels with ordinary labels hidden, portrait and landscape layouts, compact dialog scrolling, and focus return after closing. Physical-phone gesture testing remains outstanding.

3D zoom buttons and keyboard shortcuts measure distance from the active orbit centre, including after centring a selected target. Regression tests verify direction preservation, translation independence and stable minimum/maximum distances. Desktop and mobile browser checks cover centring Saturn, zooming without moving it from the centre, keyboard camera controls, and Escape returning focus to the expand button.

The enlarged reference photo and expanded 2D chart support keyboard zoom when their scroll area has focus: `+` or `=` zooms in, `-` zooms out, and `0` resets to 100%. Zoom steps by 50% and stops at 100–300%. Modified browser shortcuts and keys pressed on child controls are left alone. Regression tests cover zoom steps, bounds and unrelated keys; browser checks cover both viewers, arrow-key chart scrolling, child-button isolation, Escape focus restoration and a 390 × 844 mobile layout. Physical mobile keyboard testing remains outstanding.

Sky atlas includes Camera sky view, loaded only when opened. Start rear camera requests video from an environment-facing camera with no microphone; Enable phone motion requests orientation permission where required. Neither starts automatically. Predicted catalogue labels use current time, refreshed every 15 seconds, and the selected site. Use current location updates only the viewer until View photo & details at live time explicitly carries its site and time into the planner. Camera frames are not captured, recorded or uploaded. Closing or backgrounding the viewer stops camera tracks and orientation listeners; late permission responses cannot reopen a closed session.

Camera projection uses the [W3C device orientation rotation model](https://www.w3.org/TR/orientation-event/#worked-example), including screen rotation, with approximate adjustable vertical field of view and an uncropped video frame. Absolute orientation enables approximate compass labels; relative orientation needs alignment to a known above-horizon object. Alignment corrects bearing, with a pitch check and manual heading adjustment. Missing or invalid readings pause labels. A valid reading is retained when events go quiet, with a visible Last orientation indicator and retry action; screen rotation pauses labels until a fresh reading arrives. Manual bearing/elevation controls and object-direction preview work without a camera or sensors. Labels exclude the horizon, objects behind the camera and objects outside the viewing cone; crowding limits labels to eight and prioritises the selected object. Positions are predictions, not image recognition or guaranteed visibility. Compass drift, magnetic versus true north, lens distortion and site accuracy affect alignment.

Camera access requires a secure context and browser permission; see [getUserMedia requirements](https://developer.mozilla.org/en-US/docs/Web/API/MediaDevices/getUserMedia) and [orientation permission requirements](https://developer.mozilla.org/en-US/docs/Web/API/DeviceOrientationEvent/requestPermission_static). Camera geometry regression tests cover cardinal directions, pitch, landscape axes, projection bounds, field-of-view scaling, heading calibration and track shutdown. Local browser checks cover manual labels, selection, keyboard direction controls, no-sensor fallback, live-time detail handoff, close-focus restoration, portrait and short landscape layouts. Real rear-camera permission flows, GPS permission, iOS/Android tracking, installed PWA behavior and field alignment remain to be verified on physical phones.

Camera view also guides you towards the selected object with left/right and up/down adjustments, actual angular distance from the centre, a near-centre indication and below-horizon status. Guidance waits for a usable, aligned motion reading; manual preview instructions explicitly refer to the manual direction. Regression coverage checks north wraparound, elevation changes, targets behind the view and the small angular distances possible near zenith. A direct Retry phone motion action restarts a stalled attempt, and pending permission requests can be cancelled. Alignment pitch warnings are separate from sensor status so fresh readings do not erase them; switching mode, changing location or successful alignment clears them. Desktop/mobile checks cover guide updates, centring, below-horizon targets and no-sensor retry. Physical-device alignment and permission cancellation remain unverified.

Camera stream ownership is handled by `lib/camera-session.ts`. Cancelling, closing or backgrounding invalidates pending permission/playback work, stops active tracks and removes their listeners. Late results cannot attach or alter a newer session. Current permission and playback failures release the stream and show recovery guidance; natural camera end returns to the stopped state. Muted tracks show Camera paused, cover the interrupted video and suppress labels/direction guidance until the feed resumes. Eight session regression tests use deferred promises and track events to cover these races, interruption/resume, failed playback, natural end and hidden/unmounted viewers. Browser checks confirm the manual preview, target guidance, motion fallback and mobile layout still work. Real camera interruption/resume and permission flows still need physical-device testing.

Orientation event handling uses a monotonic clock and throttles ordinary updates to 80 ms while publishing recovery and the first absolute reading immediately. The [W3C specification](https://www.w3.org/TR/orientation-event/#deviceorientation) permits change-driven events, so four seconds without an event marks the last reading instead of discarding a stationary pose. Six regression tests cover quiet poses, missing/invalid readings, fresh readings after rotation, absolute-source preference, throttling and listener/timer cleanup. Each successful motion start changes the listener session identifier, so React batching cannot skip a retry when no permission prompt is required. Desktop and 390 × 844 browser checks cover missing/invalid-data guidance, retry, manual object preview and Escape focus restoration, with no console errors. Physical stationary-phone and screen-rotation behavior still needs device verification.

Camera heading calibration rejects objects or phone directions at 85° elevation or closer to overhead, where bearing becomes unstable, and keeps the existing 10° pitch agreement requirement. A lower object is suggested before calibration; below-horizon targets remain unavailable. Relative tracking now confirms successful alignment, and manual heading correction stays disabled until an orientation reading and usable heading are available. Two additional regression tests cover overhead, horizon and invalid inputs, pitch mismatch, north wraparound and accepted high-elevation alignment. Desktop and 390 × 844 browser checks confirm missing readings disable both calibration controls, manual Moon preview still centres its label, and Escape restores focus without overflow or console errors. Successful sensor alignment and the overhead warning still require physical-phone checks.

Camera and motion actions, target selection and manual target preview now sit above the camera stage. Permission/motion status and retry stay beside these controls. Camera settings and Back to sky view move both focus and scrolling to the destination with clearance below the sticky header. There is one target selector; preview centres the chosen above-horizon object and returns focus to the sky region. Local in-app browser checks cover desktop, 390 × 844 portrait and 844 × 390 landscape, no-sensor status, manual Moon preview, settings/return focus, Escape restoration and absence of horizontal overflow or console errors. Physical camera, touch and installed PWA testing remains outstanding.

Camera settings include per-viewer Object labels modes: All objects (default), Selected object only and Hide names. Hiding names leaves target direction guidance active; selected-only mode never substitutes another target when the chosen object is below the horizon, outside the view or near a label boundary. Status text distinguishes hidden names and missing selection from an empty view. The selector has a separate accessible name and help description. Desktop and 390 × 844 in-app browser checks verify all four visible labels reduce to Moon only, hide mode removes every label while keeping guidance, below-horizon Saturn has no label, an empty selection prompts selection, mobile settings/return focus and Escape restoration. No console errors or horizontal overflow were observed. Live-feed and physical-phone validation remains outstanding.

Camera lifecycle cleanup now covers visibility changes and pagehide; pageshow or a visible return refreshes live sky positions immediately without restarting hardware. Motion listeners are removed synchronously before a page may freeze, pending camera/motion/location work is invalidated, and obsolete sensor/calibration messages clear. Starting camera or motion also refreshes the clock. The restart notice stays beside primary controls until a new camera/motion action or manual-mode choice. Three lifecycle tests cover duplicate event ordering, history events while still visible, initially hidden viewers and listener disposal. See [MDN pagehide guidance](https://developer.mozilla.org/en-US/docs/Web/API/Window/pagehide_event) and [pageshow](https://developer.mozilla.org/en-US/docs/Web/API/Window/pageshow_event). Desktop history navigation returned through a full reload with hardware off; cached-page restoration and physical app switching remain unverified. Mobile 390 × 844 checks confirm manual Moon preview, missing-sensor recovery, clean status after manual mode and Escape focus restoration without overflow or console errors.

Camera primary controls now show the observing site, live UTC time and calculated daylight/twilight phase independently of the planner time. Check observing site scrolls to and focuses the viewer's coordinates and location control. Lighting uses geometric Sun-centre altitude without atmospheric refraction: daylight at or above 0°, with civil, nautical and astronomical twilight boundaries at −6°, −12° and −18° ([USNO definitions](https://aa.usno.navy.mil/faq/RST_defs)). This classification does not calculate conventional sunrise or sunset, weather or actual object visibility. Two regression tests cover phase boundaries, invalid altitude and Sun positions at different times and longitudes. Desktop and 390 × 844 browser checks confirm live context, site focus below the sticky header, centred manual target preview and Escape focus restoration, with no horizontal overflow or console errors. Physical camera and compass validation remains outstanding.

Phone motion now exposes Check alignment beside the primary camera controls. The shortcut scrolls to and focuses the alignment region; missing selection, missing readings and relative tracking explain why names are paused. Alignment errors stay next to the alignment action, and live tracking distinguishes approximate compass direction from object alignment. Desktop and 390 × 844 in-app browser checks cover the shortcut, disabled calibration without readings, selection, return to manual direction, centred Vega preview and Escape focus restoration. No console warnings/errors or horizontal overflow were observed. Successful compass and relative-sensor alignment still require physical-phone testing.

Camera sky view includes an opt-in Keep screen awake control. It uses the [Screen Wake Lock API](https://developer.mozilla.org/en-US/docs/Web/API/Screen_Wake_Lock_API), reports active/request/recovery state and explains the battery cost. Turning it off, closing the viewer or background/history suspension releases the lock and invalidates pending grants. Returning does not automatically request it again. Unsupported browsers and denied requests leave camera/manual controls usable. Six regression tests cover cancellation/disposal, stale grants and failures, system release, hidden viewers, already-released grants and release rejection. Desktop and 390 × 844 in-app browser checks confirm successful requests, manual turn-off, off state after reopening, accessible pressed state and mobile button wrapping without overflow or console errors. Real-phone sleep prevention, low-battery behaviour and installed PWA testing remain unverified.

Camera settings now include the shared Red light toggle, with inline help that does not cover the sky. Changes synchronize immediately between the dialog and main planner controls, including visit-only changes when storage is unavailable; storage events also handle preference removal. Newly opened controls use the current display state. Desktop and 390 × 844 in-app browser checks cover camera-to-planner synchronization, persisted red mode after reload, main-control changes reflected on reopening, centred Vega preview in red mode and mobile layout. No horizontal overflow, console warnings/errors or framework overlays were observed. Physical live-feed readability and device-brightness checks remain outstanding.

Camera location lookup now offers Keep displayed site while waiting and Use planner site after changing to device coordinates. Keeping/restoring a site invalidates pending callbacks; restoration refreshes live positions and clears heading alignment. The browser's geolocation request itself cannot be aborted, so Keep displayed site ignores its eventual result rather than dismissing a browser permission prompt. A request coordinator also rejects invalid coordinates and ignores obsolete success/error callbacks after replacement, background suspension or disposal. Four regression tests cover these races, duplicate callbacks, provider errors and coordinate validation. Desktop and 390 × 844 browser checks confirm the initial site, site shortcut focus, centred Vega preview and Escape close without overflow or console errors. GPS permission, keep-site during a real lookup and restoration after a real GPS grant still need physical-device testing.

Phone discovery correction after device testing: Start camera & discovery initiates motion permission and rear-camera acquisition from the same tap. Live camera feeds suppress fixed manual labels; an in-frame notice offers motion retry or alignment when discovery is not ready. The badge distinguishes manual preview, motion off, waiting for motion, alignment needed, discovery and last orientation. No target selection is required for all-object discovery. Safari compass readings use `webkitCompassHeading` with non-negative accuracy, aligning the full camera basis to the device top-edge heading while preserving pitch/roll ([Apple documentation](https://developer.apple.com/documentation/webkitjs/deviceorientationevent), [WebKit heading source](https://github.com/WebKit/WebKit/blob/main/Source/WebCore/platform/ios/WebCoreMotionManager.mm)). Invalid or nearly vertical top-edge headings fall back to relative orientation and known-object alignment. Recovery to compass readings clears the previous relative correction. Four added tests cover Safari turning, source loss/recovery, pitch/screen rotation and suppression of manual live-feed labels. Desktop and 390 × 844 browser checks cover revised controls, missing-sensor status, manual preview labels with no selection and no overflow or console errors. Combined permission prompts, actual compass alignment and moving labels over a live iPhone feed require another physical-device test.

Camera labels now include optional 36-pixel reference thumbnails, using the existing NASA spacecraft images and DSS star fields. Thumbnail failures fall back to an icon while the name stays usable; camera settings can disable thumbnails. Photo labels reserve more space for bounds and collision checks, and source credits remain available through selected-object photo details. Full screen camera fills the app viewport while retaining exit, discovery/cancel, motion/cancel and settings actions. Settings returns focus to the settings region; Escape exits full-screen mode before closing the camera dialog. Camera and sensor sessions remain mounted when switching layouts. Video stays uncropped, with projected labels fitted to the actual image rectangle inside portrait/landscape letterboxing. Browser chrome may remain visible; an installed PWA uses its standalone viewport. A regression test covers fitted image geometry and projection centres. Desktop, 390 × 844 portrait and 844 × 390 landscape browser checks confirm a loaded Vega thumbnail, visible controls, thumbnail toggling, settings focus, Escape handling and no overflow or console errors. Live-camera fullscreen, device rotation and iPhone safe-area behaviour still need physical testing.

Safari discovery stability: after a usable compass pose establishes north, nearly upright poses retain that north correction while every fresh gyro pose continues to move the projection. A brief compass-quality failure uses the same correction for at most 500 ms after the last valid compass event; sustained failure returns to relative alignment. This preserves direction tracking rather than freezing old label positions. An upright initial pose still needs a slightly tilted valid compass pose or known-object alignment, and invalid orientation clears the cached north correction. Three new regression tests cover upright turning, short/sustained compass loss, recovery and missing/invalid initial anchors. Physical iPhone flicker behaviour requires retesting; objects still enter and leave the field of view normally.

Camera label placement stability: existing visible names keep priority over brighter newcomers when labels overlap. New names require an extra eight pixels of clearance at image edges and collisions; retained names use the normal safe bounds. This creates separate entry/exit thresholds without retaining stale coordinates or showing clipped labels. Selecting a target takes immediate priority, and hidden names, unavailable orientation and targets outside the projection clear the visible-name history. Five regression tests cover edge thresholds, collision priority, selection, fresh coordinates, removal, limits and thumbnail widths. All 97 tests, type checking, lint and production build pass. Local 390 × 844 portrait and 844 × 390 landscape browser checks cover centred Vega with a loaded thumbnail, full-screen controls, hidden/selected/all modes, thumbnail toggling and no overflow or console errors. These previews do not validate physical iPhone compass tracking or live-camera flicker.

Dual orientation stream handling: after standard absolute compass data is preferred, secondary relative events are ignored before validation. An invalid relative event can no longer erase a good compass pose, and secondary events cannot reset the compass quiet timer or restore the pose after screen rotation. Invalid events from the preferred stream still pause labels. Two additional event-sequence regression tests cover these cases; existing Safari compass tests continue to exercise its separate heading path. This addresses browsers emitting both standard orientation streams; it is not evidence that physical iPhone flicker is resolved.

Safari events with omitted compass fields: once Safari's heading stream is identified, a primary motion event without those fields is treated as a compass dropout rather than ignored as secondary relative data. Fresh gyro positions continue moving the projection within the existing 500 ms grace period; sustained absence returns to relative alignment, and a valid heading restores compass discovery. No north anchor is fabricated on initial upright poses, and invalid gyro data still clears the anchor. Two added event-sequence tests cover omission, movement, expiry, recovery and invalid readings. Physical iPhone behavior remains to be retested.

Upright compass correction now uses separate suspension/re-entry thresholds. When the device top-edge horizontal projection drops below 0.1, heading correction is suspended until that projection reaches 0.2 (roughly 12 degrees away from vertical). The existing north anchor continues to rotate fresh gyro poses, so motion is not frozen. The compass-loss grace period still applies, and invalid gyro data clears the anchor and threshold state. Two regression tests cover noisy headings around the cutoff, fresh turning, screen rotation, correction resumption and invalid-data reset. These synthetic event tests do not establish physical iPhone compass accuracy.

Final motion-pose delivery: orientation rendering still limits updates to an 80 ms interval, but now coalesces intervening events and delivers the newest pose at the end of that interval. Stopping the phone between updates no longer leaves labels at a discarded earlier position. Rotation, invalid readings, source-transition refresh and disposal cancel queued work; secondary ignored streams do not replace it. Three added regression tests cover final-pose delivery, cancellation and immediate absolute-source precedence. The quiet indicator continues to use the last sensor-event time rather than the deferred render time. Physical camera tracking remains a separate phone test.

Full-screen discovery recovery: Check alignment now exits full-screen layout and focuses the visible alignment panel instead of focusing a hidden element. A direct alignment action is also available in the full-screen toolbar while motion is enabled. The footer shows camera/motion errors, pending permission, session notices or the current label status; hidden names and empty views no longer leave an unexplained blank overlay. Waiting for direction suppresses the manual bearing readout, and unaligned relative headings are labelled relative. Local 390 × 844 portrait and 844 × 390 landscape checks cover hidden-name status, desktop no-sensor errors, toolbar controls and alignment focus with no overflow or console errors. Real camera permission errors and physical-device alignment remain to be verified on phones.

Selected-label edge stability: selecting an object still gives it first priority over overlapping labels, but no longer bypasses the wider entry margin at image edges. After a selected label leaves its safe bounds, it must move eight pixels farther into the image before returning; an already visible label continues to use the narrower exit margin and fresh coordinates. Direction guidance remains available when the label is outside those bounds. A new multi-step regression test follows a selected object through edge jitter, exit and re-entry.

Calibration reference changes: sustained Safari compass loss now clears north-based object alignment and heading correction before delivering the first relative-motion pose. This prevents an offset calibrated in the compass frame being reused against a different gyro bearing reference. The brief compass grace period preserves the current correction; ordinary relative readings preserve a new known-object alignment, and subsequent compass recovery clears it as before. Two regression tests cover callback order, correction retention within grace, one reset per source transition and unchanged relative sessions. Physical-phone compass loss and re-alignment still require field testing.

Review fixes: Tonight's above-horizon shortcuts now select the chosen object before opening Sky atlas, including when another target was already selected. Main planner location lookup reuses the tested request coordinator: choosing a searched/saved/manual site or Keep selected site invalidates pending GPS callbacks, and disposal ignores late results. The browser location prompt itself cannot be cancelled. Production builds stamp the emitted service worker with a deterministic fingerprint of the client bundles, worker source and offline page, so ordinary UI releases change worker bytes at the existing stable URL and can trigger the update prompt. Two new tests cover unchanged/reordered builds, app-only changes and offline/worker changes. All 111 tests, type checking, lint and production build pass. Local 390 × 844 and 1280 × 900 browser checks confirm Vega → Tonight → Saturn opens Saturn without overflow or console errors. The actual waiting-worker prompt appeared in the local preview; Update and reload preserved observing time and saved Vega notes. Real GPS races and an installed iPhone PWA upgrading on the deployed origin still need device testing.

Camera status-badge clearance: label placement now excludes the measured badge rectangle in stage coordinates, including selected objects and letterboxed full-screen views. A ResizeObserver tracks badge/stage size, and switching full-screen layout refreshes its position. Entry clearance is wider than retained-label clearance to resist jitter; labels remain at their predicted positions rather than being shifted away from the object. Two regression tests cover overlap, selected/retained priority, entry/exit margins and changing badge bounds. All 113 tests, type checking, lint and production build pass. In the local preview, selecting Polaris and reducing manual elevation from 30° to 27° suppressed its badge-overlapping label; centring it restored the name and thumbnail. Browser checks at 390 × 844, 844 × 390 and 1280 × 900 covered full-screen switching and dialog close without overflow, framework overlays or console errors. No camera/GPS permissions were granted; live iPhone tracking remains unverified.

Remembered camera preferences: the vertical field of view (30–100°, integer degrees) and reference-thumbnail toggle are saved on this browser and restored when reopening the viewer. Corrupt or unavailable storage uses safe defaults, valid fields survive an invalid sibling field, and write failures show a session-only notice. Camera/motion activation, heading calibration, manual direction and label mode remain session settings. Check field of view again when changing camera lens or zoom. Two regression tests cover reopen persistence, bounds, corrupt storage, blocked reads/writes and exclusion of calibration data. All 115 tests, type checking, lint and production build pass. Local 390 × 844 and 1280 × 900 checks verified 61° with thumbnails off after close/reopen and page reload, with no overflow, framework overlay or console errors. Preview preferences were restored to 60° and thumbnails on. No hardware permissions were requested; physical-phone camera testing remains outstanding.

Full-screen target selection: the camera footer now includes the same Object to follow picker as the normal viewer. Both pickers group targets above/below the horizon and order them by live altitude, highest first. Explore all objects clears the followed target without changing the label-display setting. Choosing an off-screen target keeps full-screen layout and direction guidance available; below-horizon objects remain selectable with explicit guidance. This changes selection within the mounted viewer rather than remounting its camera session. All 115 tests, type checking, lint and production build pass. Local 390 × 844, 844 × 390 and 1280 × 900 checks covered Polaris selection in full screen, Moon below-horizon guidance, clearing selection and preserving Polaris when exiting full screen. The landscape view retained 207 pixels of camera-stage height with the footer inside the viewport; no overflow, framework overlays or console errors were observed. Hardware permissions were not granted, so live-camera continuity on iPhone remains a device test.

Camera lens scale through image rotation: the field-of-view slider now calibrates the image's longer side. Projection derives the vertical angle from the actual video aspect ratio; for a 60° long-side angle, a 16:9 image has about 36° vertical field of view rather than 60°. Viewport letterboxing alone does not change the lens angle. This assumes the same lens and uncropped image through rotation; zoom, cropping and lens changes still need calibration. The settings panel shows the derived vertical angle. Settings use a new storage version: older vertical-angle values default to 60° because their calibration orientation was not stored, while thumbnail preferences are retained. Two added tests cover axis-swapped projection invariance, invalid geometry and settings migration. All 117 tests, type checking, lint and production build pass. Local 390 × 844, 844 × 390 and 1280 × 900 browser checks cover slider changes, reopen persistence, full-screen layout and settings return without overflow, overlays or console errors. No hardware permissions were granted: actual rear-video metadata during rotation and physical iPhone alignment still require phone testing.

Camera recovery actions: Reset camera preferences restores a 60° lens field of view, thumbnails and all-label mode, saves the persistent preferences and preserves the followed target and heading correction. Clear heading correction in the alignment panel clears the alignment flag, error and bearing offset without stopping camera or motion. Compass-based labels can continue; relative-motion labels require a new known-object alignment under the existing label gate. The reset notice is neutral so later compass recovery does not leave stale alignment instructions. All 117 tests, type checking, lint and production build pass. Local 390 × 844, 844 × 390 and 1280 × 900 checks exercised altered FOV/thumbnails/hidden labels → reset → 60°/photos/all labels, followed-target preservation, reopened defaults and full-screen Settings return, with no overflow, overlays or console errors. No hardware permissions were granted. Clearing an active absolute or relative heading correction and live-feed continuity still need physical-phone testing.

Camera playback interruptions: track mute and HTML video playback now have independent pause state. The overlay pauses if either interrupts an active camera. A media-event watcher checks current stream, pause and frame readiness on pause/waiting/stalled/playing/canplay/emptied events; queued events cannot overwrite a recovered player's actual state, detached streams clear the playback pause and disposal removes listeners. Camera-off manual preview is unaffected. Three regression tests cover stopped playback, frame starvation, recovery, duplicate/stale events, detach and cleanup. All 120 tests, type checking, lint and production build pass. Local 390 × 844, 844 × 390 and 1280 × 900 browser checks cover centred Polaris manual preview, full-screen target selection, Settings return and dialog close without overflow, overlays or console errors. No hardware permissions were granted. The tests exercise synthetic media-event sequences; actual video pause/buffering and recovery over a live iPhone feed remain unverified.

Camera thumbnail loading: pending reference images now show the star fallback immediately instead of a dark empty square. The image remains hidden until its load event, replaces the icon on success and retains the icon on failure. Absolute image positioning keeps the thumbnail at 36 × 36 pixels in either state. All 120 tests, type checking, lint and production build pass. Local 390 × 844, 844 × 390 and 1280 × 900 browser checks observed a pending Polaris image with a visible fallback, a loaded 800-pixel Moon source with the icon removed, unchanged thumbnail bounds and working thumbnail toggles without overflow, overlays or console errors. No hardware permissions were granted; photo loading was tested in manual preview.

Full-screen screen awake: the camera toolbar now exposes the existing optional wake-lock action, including pending cancellation and explicit turn-off. The footer shows active/requesting status or release/failure notices with an accessible description. Normal and full-screen layouts share one wake-lock session and the same action/help text. All 120 tests, type checking, lint and production build pass. Local 390 × 844, 844 × 390 and 1280 × 900 browser checks obtained an actual browser wake-lock grant, showed aria-pressed=true and active help, released it explicitly, reacquired it and confirmed active state after exiting full screen before releasing again. The landscape footer stayed inside the 390-pixel viewport with 167 pixels of stage height; no overflow, overlays or console errors were observed. Camera/GPS permissions were not requested. iPhone power-management behaviour and wake-lock release while live discovery is running still require phone testing.

Manual camera preview rotation: when the camera is off, labels now project into the whole preview canvas instead of a fitted portrait-video rectangle. This fixes centred thumbnail labels disappearing in full-screen landscape because the old frame was narrower than the label. Starting/live camera sessions still use the fitted video rectangle. A regression test covers centred-label placement, retained live-video bounds and zero-size stages. All 121 tests, type checking, lint and production build pass. Local 390 × 844 portrait, 844 × 390 landscape and 1280 × 900 desktop browser checks confirmed Polaris stays visible after rotation and exiting full screen, without horizontal overflow, framework overlays or console warnings/errors. Hardware permissions were not requested; actual iPhone video rotation and alignment remain device tests.

Deep Sky Central-inspired development: the first addition is a separate Deep-sky explorer in Sky atlas, with 109 Messier entries adapted from OpenNGC under CC BY-SA 4.0 (provenance and transformations in `data/README.md`). Search common names, Messier or NGC identifiers (spaces and leading zeros supported), filter by object type, constellation, maximum visual magnitude or altitude strictly above 30°, and rank by current altitude. Selected details show V magnitude, catalogue angular dimensions and J2000 coordinates. Positions are precessed to the selected date. The best-time action picks the highest 15-minute sample above 30° with the Sun below −18° over the next 24 hours and updates the shared planner time. This does not predict weather or guaranteed visibility. Missing catalogue values remain absent; M102 is not assigned. Deep-sky entries are separate from the bright-star atlas/camera and saved-target system in this first release. The reference's larger catalogue, session diary, checklists, AI assistant, framing-image tools and gallery are not implemented by this addition.

Validation: 124 tests pass, including search/filter cases, a precession cross-check against Astronomy Engine's defined-star calculation and darkness/altitude recommendation boundaries. Type checking, lint and production build pass. Local production browser checks at 1280 × 900 and 390 × 844 covered NGC 224 → Andromeda, incompatible-type empty state, Pleiades selection, M 31 search, above-30° filtering and recommended-time propagation (London 6 Oct 00:45 local → 5 Oct 23:45 UTC). No console warnings/errors, framework overlays or horizontal overflow were observed. No camera/GPS permissions requested. Catalogue browsing uses bundled data; full planner reopening offline and physical-phone performance remain unverified.

Deep-sky reference imagery and altitude planning: selected Messier details now include a collapsible DSS red-band reference image with the existing enlarged zoom viewer, retry state and source credits. Fields use the catalogue J2000 centre and a 15–60 arcminute square, sized from the listed major axis. Objects larger than the 60-arcminute DSS limit are explicitly labelled central-region views. Target changes reset the disclosure and photo state. The same 97 samples (15 minutes over 24 hours) now feed the best-time recommendation and an altitude curve with darkness shading, a 30° guide and 25 hourly buttons. Hour buttons show dates and UTC offsets, update the shared planner time and start a new rolling 24-hour window. Mobile uses a two-column hourly grid. This is not image recognition, live imagery or a visibility guarantee.

Validation: all 126 tests, type checking, lint and production build pass. Added tests cover source coordinates, field limits and central-region captions, uninterrupted UTC sample spacing through the repeated London autumn clock-change hour, and strict darkness/altitude boundaries. Local production browser checks at 1280 × 900 and 390 × 844 verified M31 image disclosure, a loaded 3564-pixel DSS image, enlarged zoom to 150%, mobile caption scrolling, hour selection (6 Oct 00:00 London → 5 Oct 23:00 UTC), 25 hourly controls, two mobile columns and M57 selection resetting the photo disclosure and chart. No horizontal overflow, framework overlays or console warnings/errors observed. Photo failure/retry remains the existing shared implementation; it was not forced in this browser run. Hardware permissions were not requested. Image availability depends on STScI/network access, and physical-phone performance remains unverified.

Deep-sky imaging handoff: Plan imaging for a selected Messier object opens Photo tools, initializes its J2000 centre coordinates and focuses/scrolls to the mosaic planner. A catalogue picker within that planner can change targets while retaining the mounted planner's equipment, overlap, grid dimensions and rotation. Catalogue metadata shows the selected object and angular extent; manually editing either coordinate clears that identity and switches the picker to Manual coordinates. An equipment shortcut returns to the existing setup controls. This adds coordinate handoff and a target picker, not an image-based framing overlay. As before, switching away from Photo tools unmounts its form; unsaved setup/grid edits are not retained across tab changes. Saved equipment profiles remain available to reload. The last explorer handoff remains available for the current page visit, but is not persisted in backups or local storage.

Validation: all 126 tests, type checking, lint and production build pass. Local production checks at 1280 × 900 and 390 × 844 covered explorer M45 → Photo tools with exact source coordinates and mosaic focus, target change to M57 retaining 600 mm focal length / 3 × 2 grid / 35% overlap / 15° rotation, manual RA edit clearing the catalogue identity, selecting M31 again and mobile navigation → M42 handoff with negative declination. No horizontal overflow, framework overlays or console warnings/errors observed. Existing mosaic calculations and export tests pass; no new computation was introduced. Physical-phone native picker behaviour and acquisition-software imports remain unverified.

Imaging draft recovery supersedes the earlier tab-unmount limitation: the planner now owns equipment inputs, manual/catalogue centre, grid, overlap, rotation and guiding RMS as one draft. Editing saves a versioned `nightjar-imaging-draft-v1` snapshot separately from saved equipment profiles. Opening imaging from the explorer changes only the draft's target coordinates and catalogue identity; the focus handoff is consumed once so ordinary tab returns do not jump to the mosaic. Empty and unfinished numerical edits remain recoverable and continue to use existing calculation validation. Catalogue metadata is displayed only when the coordinates actually match the chosen source entry. A bounded schema rejects malformed/oversized/future-version data, retains only known fields and leaves an invalid stored snapshot untouched until an edit. Storage errors keep the in-memory draft available across tabs for the current visit and show a visit-only status. Drafts are not included in the existing plan backup, and separate open tabs are not synchronized (the last successful edit wins for future reloads).

Validation: all 129 tests, type checking, lint and production build pass. Three new tests cover unfinished draft round trips without modifying equipment-profile storage, corrupt/future/oversized data recovery, blocked storage and independent default copies. Local production checks at 1280 × 900 and 390 × 844 covered 650 mm / 3 × 2 / 35% overlap / 15° rotation → explorer M57 handoff with settings retained, reload recovery, empty focal-length recovery with validation, manual-coordinate identity surviving a tab round trip and mobile navigation preserving focal length/grid. No horizontal overflow, framework overlays or console warnings/errors observed. Storage failure was tested with a synthetic storage adapter, not a forced browser-denial state. Physical-phone storage eviction, private-mode behaviour and acquisition-software import remain unverified.

Angular framing guide: the mosaic preview now shows rotated camera-panel polygons and the selected target's angular outline on one north-up, east-right tangent plane. Catalogue data now also retains OpenNGC major-axis position angles in degrees east of north. Known major/minor axes and position angle produce an ellipse; incomplete orientation/axis data uses an explicitly labelled major-axis bounding circle. Fit messages compare a centred single-frame rectangle and the schematic mosaic's outer bounds. These are catalogue-size comparisons, not image overlays, exact object boundaries, visibility predictions or acquisition commands. Canvas bounds include the full rotated grid and object outline so large objects remain visible. Changing to manual coordinates removes the catalogue outline. Existing CSV coordinate export is unchanged.

Validation: all 132 tests, type checking, lint and production build pass. Three new tests cross-check visual panel centres against exported spherical mosaic coordinates at multiple rotations, test orientation/overlap effects on ellipse containment and conservative unknown-angle bounds, and reject invalid grid geometry. Local production checks at 1280 × 900 and 390 × 844 covered M31 at 650 mm, 1 × 1 / 0° extending beyond the frame, 1 × 1 / 125° fitting a centred frame, 2 × 2 / 35° fitting mosaic bounds, four visible rotated polygons and a 35° catalogue ellipse, M57's bounding-circle explanation and manual coordinates clearing the outline. No horizontal overflow, framework overlays or console warnings/errors observed. Physical-phone rendering and acquisition-software framing/import remain unverified.


Saved deep-sky shortlists: Messier objects can now be saved separately from bright-star favourites, selected from a highest-altitude-first list and removed individually. The validated, deduplicated browser list synchronizes between open tabs and survives reloads. Storage failures retain visit-only edits with a status message. A standalone HTML observing-plan download includes selected site/time, snapshot altitude and azimuth, J2000 coordinates, catalogue properties, equipment and source attribution, with printable checkboxes and no remote resources. It is a snapshot rather than a data backup. The PWA offline page shows saved Messier IDs, with reconnect guidance rather than stale positions.

Plan backups now export version 2 with deep-sky IDs, accept version 1 files and normalize legacy data to an empty deep-sky list. Preview/import combine existing and incoming IDs; the five-key transaction rolls back completed writes on failure. New backups require an updated app; imaging drafts remain outside plan backups.

Validation: all 136 tests, type checking, lint and production build pass. New tests cover catalogue ID validation, deduplication, blocked/corrupt storage, all 109 printable entries, HTML escaping, version 1/2 compatibility, merged IDs, five-key rollback and offline list validation. Browser checks at 390 × 844 verified M31/M45 save and reload persistence, highest-first ordering, saved-target selection and no horizontal overflow. The downloaded HTML file was found in Downloads after the browser download-event waiter timed out. The offline page directly rendered both saved IDs. This does not verify service-worker offline navigation or physical-phone storage behaviour.

Deep-sky observing diary: Sky atlas now records Seen / Not seen / Imaged observations for the selected Messier object, with optional equipment and up to 2,000 characters of notes. Saving captures the selected UTC instant and site, rather than assuming the current time. Editing retains that original snapshot. Each target has an in-memory unfinished form while the explorer is mounted; leaving Sky atlas or reloading discards unfinished forms. Saved entries survive reloads, synchronize between tabs, and can be filtered by selected object or shown together, newest observation first, in batches of ten. The first version supports adding and editing up to 200 records; deletion, photos and diary export to PDF are not implemented. Corrupt storage disables saving without overwriting it; failed writes retain form text for retry.

Backups export version 3 with the diary, import versions 1–3 and normalize older files without diary data to an empty incoming collection. Merging keeps existing versions of matching entry IDs and reports changed incoming conflicts in the preview. The six-key restore transaction includes diary rollback. The offline page renders saved observation time, site name, outcome, equipment and notes as text; it does not recalculate sky positions.

Validation: 139 tests, type checking, lint and production build pass. New tests cover bounded diary schema, unique IDs, invalid calendar dates, multiline text, corrupt/blocked storage, version 1/2 compatibility, diary merging/conflict previews and offline text rendering. Existing rollback coverage now exercises all six writes. Local production browser checks at 1280 × 900 and 390 × 844 verified M31 save, reload persistence, edit to Not seen after advancing planner time while retaining the original timestamp/site, all-object filtering from M45, focus/scroll to the edit form, and no horizontal overflow or console warnings/errors. A downloaded version 3 backup was inspected for the edited entry and original instant. Direct navigation to the offline page displayed the entry. Physical-phone storage and service-worker offline navigation remain unverified.

Saved deep-sky comparison: a collapsible panel now compares up to six saved Messier objects across the next 24 hours. It calculates only while open, shares Sun-altitude samples across targets and memoizes by selection, observing instant and coordinates. Qualifying rows require a sample strictly above 30° with Sun altitude at or below −18°, and rank by highest eligible altitude. Cards show the best sampled time, qualifying intervals and their total duration. Intervals count only adjacent qualifying 15-minute samples; isolated eligible peaks remain available but do not create a duration. Objects without eligible samples have an explicit no-opportunity message. A horizontally scrollable hourly table enables only qualifying cells, with UTC offsets and a +24-hour endpoint. Choosing a card or cell selects the explorer object and updates shared planner time, starting a new rolling window. This is astronomical planning, not a weather, Moon-glare or visibility forecast. Comparison selection is temporary; the underlying shortlist remains saved and backed up.

Validation: 142 tests, type checking, lint and production build pass. New tests cross-check all comparison samples against the single-object planner, verify descending eligible peaks, strict threshold boundaries, isolated samples, non-adjacent gaps, split intervals, six-target limits, polar no-darkness cases and clock-change absolute durations. Local browser checks at 1280 × 900 and 390 × 844 verified M31/M45 ordering, Pleiades best-time handoff (6 Oct 03:45 London → 02:45 UTC), deselection empty state, London solstice no-darkness state, mobile single-column cards, horizontal timeline keyboard scrolling and a qualifying M31 hour-cell handoff (5 Oct 22:00 London → 21:00 UTC). No horizontal document overflow, framework overlays or console warnings/errors observed. Physical-phone performance and touch scrolling remain unverified.

Moon context for deep-sky planning: object details now show the lunar phase, illuminated fraction, apparent Moon altitude and angular separation at the selected time and at the existing altitude-based recommended time. The separation uses both apparent horizon directions and a clamped spherical angular-distance calculation. Illuminated fraction uses Astronomy Engine's geocentric Illumination phase_fraction. These values provide context rather than a sky-brightness or contrast prediction. The original single-object recommendation remains altitude-based.

Saved-target comparison now adds Moon context at each best sample, hourly Moon-up/down labels and an optional Require Moon below horizon filter. Shared Moon-altitude samples are calculated once per time across compared objects. Enabling the filter recomputes best times, eligible intervals, row order and selectable hourly cells while retaining altitude strictly above 30° and Sun altitude at or below −18°. Moon-down means the apparent Moon centre is at or below 0° on the ideal horizon; the lunar limb and local terrain are not modelled. No glare severity scores are assigned. This temporary filter is not saved in backups.

Validation: all 145 tests, type checking, lint and production build pass. New tests compare angular separation with Astronomy Engine vector angles, cover azimuth wraparound/identical/opposite/zenith directions, check the selected Moon geometry and illumination, and verify Moon-down boundary filtering without weakening existing darkness/altitude rules. Comparison regression tests preserve agreement with single-object sky samples. Local production browser checks at 1280 × 900 and 390 × 844 verified M45's unfiltered 6 Oct 03:45 London recommendation (Moon altitude 16.7°) shifting to 01:30 (Moon altitude −1.5°), shortened intervals and filtered handoff to 00:30 UTC. On 26 Oct, Moon-down filtering correctly returned no eligible M31/M45 sample; removing it showed M31 best-time context with a 99%-illuminated Moon at 52.0° altitude and 32.5° separation. Mobile detail panels fit without horizontal overflow, framework overlays or console warnings/errors. Physical-phone performance and real-world observing conditions remain unverified.

Diary downloads: the observing diary now exports a standalone print-ready HTML report and UTF-8 CSV for the current object filter or all objects. Exports include all saved entries in that filter, independent of the ten-entry display pagination, and exclude unfinished new/edit forms. Empty-filter buttons are disabled. Both formats retain original observation instants, site/time-zone details, outcome, equipment and multiline notes, newest first. HTML contains no remote resources, escapes user text and has responsive/print styles plus a Print or save as PDF button. CSV uses a UTF-8 BOM, quoted text, escaped double quotes and CRLF row endings; formula-like text is prefixed with an apostrophe for spreadsheet safety. Numeric coordinates retain their stored precision. Downloads are snapshots and cannot replace Nightjar backup imports.

Validation: all 148 tests, type checking, lint and production build pass. Three new tests cover all 200 report entries, HTML escaping/no remote resources, Unicode/comma/quote/multiline CSV round trips, repeated London clock-change hours with explicit UTC offsets, formula-like text, empty exports and invalid diary data. Local production browser checks at 1280 × 900 and 390 × 844 verified downloads, disabled exports for M45's empty filter, all-object exports including M31, and exclusion of unfinished form notes. Downloaded HTML and CSV files were inspected for original timestamps and complete multiline notes. The downloaded report was rendered by a temporary loopback server outside the repository and visually checked at desktop/mobile sizes without overflow; app console checks were clean. Native PDF printing, spreadsheet application imports and physical-phone download handling remain unverified.

Diary search and filters: saved observations can now be searched by Messier/common/NGC name, observing site, equipment or note text, combined with result and an inclusive UTC date range. Search normalizes Unicode compatibility forms, case, whitespace and spaced/zero-padded catalogue identifiers. Results remain newest first and show a matching/total count for the selected object scope. Clear diary filters resets search/result/dates while retaining the selected-object/all-objects scope. Downloads use the complete matching set, not only the displayed page. Reversed or invalid calendar dates show a validation message and disable downloads; an empty match has its own recovery guidance. Date entry uses explicit YYYY-MM-DD text with a mobile numeric keyboard and automatic conversion of complete YYYYMMDD input, replacing native date fields that did not retain entered values during browser QA. Filters are temporary and do not change saved records or observing time.

Validation: all 152 tests, type checking, lint and production build pass. Four new tests cover common/catalogue identifiers, equipment/notes/Unicode searches, result combinations, inclusive UTC boundaries despite different local dates, source immutability, calendar/reversed-range errors and eight-digit mobile date normalization. Local production checks at 1280 × 900 and 390 × 844 used two labelled QA observations: an M31 Not seen record on 5 Oct and an M45 Seen record on 6 Oct. Combined NGC 224 / Not seen / 5 Oct filters selected only M31; a downloaded CSV was inspected for exactly that entry. Reversing the range produced a visible error and disabled export. Clearing restored both records, and Pleiades / Seen / numeric 6 Oct inputs selected M45 on mobile without overflow or console warnings/errors. Physical-phone keyboard behaviour and native spreadsheet imports remain unverified.

PWA navigation recovery: the service worker now aborts navigation requests that have not returned a response within eight seconds and serves the cached offline page on network failure, timeout or HTTP server errors (5xx). Successful pages, redirects and real client errors such as 404/401 remain unchanged. If cached storage is unavailable, a plain 503 recovery message is returned. Offline-cache version is bumped to v5; old Nightjar caches are removed on activation. Existing immutable-asset caching stays capped at 80 files and continues to exclude APIs/live forecasts, query-bearing assets, authorized requests, private/no-store responses and foreign origins. The offline page's online-status message now explains that the planner may be slow or temporarily unavailable.

Validation: all 156 tests, type checking, lint and production build pass. Four new service-worker tests cover server/network/storage errors, the eight-second abort and timer cleanup, install HTML validation, activation cleanup and bounded static-cache exclusions. An isolated loopback test origin loaded the actual worker source through normal browser registration and seeded labelled QA records. In-app browser navigation to a real 503 response recovered to the cached offline page with M31 and its diary note. A real 404 remained a 404 page, and retry returned to the healthy page. A server endpoint delaying its response for 12 seconds also recovered to the cached page. The browser automation's navigation waiter timed out during that stalled request; a subsequent DOM read confirmed the completed fallback, so exact browser wall-clock timing is not claimed. Mobile 390 × 844 rendering retained the saved records without horizontal overflow. The test worker/cache was removed through the test UI. Physical iPhone flight-mode navigation, installed-PWA lifecycle and Cloudflare production failures still need device/deployment validation; reopening the full planner offline is not supported by this fallback.

Diary draft recovery supersedes the earlier unfinished-form limitation. A versioned, bounded browser snapshot now retains one unfinished new observation per Messier object and one active edit. First input captures original observing site/time; changing planner time or navigating does not silently move the observation. An explicit Use current site and time action updates the draft metadata while retaining text. Reload/navigation restore new/edit forms, including multiline notes and result/equipment. New draft IDs become saved record IDs, so failed cleanup cannot silently duplicate an already-saved observation; identical completed drafts are ignored on recovery. Clear unfinished form has a visit-local Undo action. Saved observations and pending forms remain separate; drafts are excluded from backups, offline listings and report/CSV downloads. Drafts are browser-local rather than collaborative: multiple tabs can overwrite the shared pending-form snapshot; saved-entry edits compare against the latest record and reject an older original, retaining text for review. This is a stale-edit check, not an atomic cross-tab transaction.

Validation: all 160 tests, type checking, lint and production build pass. Four new tests cover bounded draft schema, metadata invariants, complete new/edit round trips, Unicode/multiline text, saved-diary isolation, corrupt/future/oversized data, denied/quota storage, and completed-draft suppression without discarding differing text. Local production browser checks covered navigation to Moon, planner-time changes, return to Sky atlas, reload, explicit snapshot replacement, clear/undo, edit restoration, and a second-tab saved change causing rejection of the original tab's stale edit. Mobile 390 × 844 checks retained text without overflow; saving the new QA draft preserved its explicit 7 Oct timestamp and reload showed an empty form, with no duplicate observation or console errors. Draft storage access is now guarded even when the localStorage getter itself fails. Physical-phone eviction/private-mode behaviour and simultaneous cross-tab writes remain unverified.

Release validation: `.github/workflows/validate.yml` runs on main pushes, pull requests and manual dispatch. It installs committed npm dependencies using the existing `.node-version`, then runs `npm run verify`: type checking, lint, all automated tests, production build and Cloudflare packaging dry-run. The job uses read-only repository permissions, cancels superseded runs and has a 20-minute timeout. It does not publish or require deployment credentials. Cloudflare's documented build command in `CLOUDFLARE.md` is now `npm run verify`; an independently configured Workers Build must use that command to run these checks before its deploy step. The GitHub workflow was parsed locally and its trigger/command/permission structure checked. Existing local verification stages pass and Wrangler dry-run produced a 1,859 KiB package (532 KiB gzip) with no bindings. The hosted GitHub job and fresh Linux dependency installation remain pending the user's batch push; no workflow or deployment was triggered remotely.

Bounded town search: browser location searches now have a 15-second deadline and a visible Cancel location search action. Timeout clears the busy state, preserves the selected observing site/time, and offers retry or coordinates. User cancellation stays separate from timeout; an aborted older request cannot replace a newer result. The helper guards malformed JSON, validates location fields/finite IDs/optional region labels and limits displayed results to six. It clears its timer and external abort listener on completion and rejects a late transport response even if the transport ignores abort.

Validation: all 165 tests, type checking, lint and production build pass. Five new tests cover Unicode/trimmed query encoding, provider result validation/caps, timeout and retry on the same caller signal, external/pre-cancelled requests, invalid queries, provider errors, malformed JSON and ignored-abort late responses. Browser QA used the actual built app through an isolated loopback proxy with 18-second delayed search responses. Cancel returned to an enabled Search action without changing the site; a subsequent stalled request showed the timeout message and enabled retry. Switching the fixture to successful search produced a labelled London QA result. Desktop 1280 × 900 and mobile 390 × 844 checks showed no overflow or console warnings/errors. Provider production outages and actual phone network loss remain unverified.

Reference-photo recovery: a photo visible in the viewport for 15 seconds without finishing now offers Retry photo and source-link guidance. The existing image request remains mounted, allowing a late successful load to remove the message automatically. Hidden lazy images do not start the delay timer; hiding/unmounting cancels pending work, and each attempt offers recovery only once. Failed photos retain the retry action. Images remain visually hidden until their load event, and source changes remount image state. Recovery cards can grow beyond the normal photo aspect ratio on small screens. The enlarged viewer scrolls its full content when necessary; recovery text uses the available viewport width instead of being constrained to the zoom canvas's square size.

Validation: all 168 tests, type checking, lint and production build pass. Three new tests cover visible/hidden timer behavior, one-shot delay offers, observer fallback, unmount cancellation and queued callbacks after disposal. Browser QA used the real photo component through an isolated loopback proxy replacing only DSS request URLs with labelled test fixtures. A 28-second image response showed the delay message at 15 seconds and later completed without retry. A failed response showed retry, and changing the fixture to success loaded the image after retry. Mobile 390 × 844 and small-phone 320 × 568 checks confirmed readable messages and accessible controls/source links without document overflow; the small enlarged-viewer recovery canvas used the full 269-pixel available width. The fixture's successful image was a one-pixel GIF, so no astronomical-image authenticity or real provider performance is claimed. Test worker/cache registrations and servers were cleaned up. Physical-phone photo downloads and old-browser CSS fallbacks remain device checks.

Weather API coordinate validation: blank and whitespace-only query values no longer become numeric zero. Requests now require valid finite decimal/scientific coordinate values within latitude ±90° and longitude ±180°, while preserving legitimate zero and boundary values. Missing, malformed, hexadecimal and out-of-range coordinates return 400 before contacting Open-Meteo. Three route/parser tests verify rejected requests make no provider call and valid zero coordinates preserve the existing response/cache contract. All 171 tests, type checking, lint and production build pass. The generated TypeScript incremental cache is now ignored and removed from version control while remaining available locally, preventing routine checks from dirtying the checkout.


Observing-time input boundaries: invalid zones and incomplete/invalid dates now return no candidate rather than raising an input-handler exception. Years are padded for the form, year zero is rejected, and offset samples crossing the four-digit year range are ignored instead of creating Invalid Date values. Four new tests cover UTC/quarter-hour zones, London spring gaps/autumn repeated hours, four-digit year boundaries and invalid input/zone handling. All 175 tests, type checking, lint and production build pass. These parser checks do not extend the astronomy model's physical accuracy to arbitrary historical or far-future dates.

Deep-sky browsing also accepts full constellation names in search and Unicode-compatible catalogue identifiers. Constellation labels use the [IAU names](https://iauarchive.eso.org/public/themes/constellations/), with OpenNGC’s Se1/Se2 split shown as Serpens Caput/Cauda. Magnitude presets filter integrated V magnitude; they do not promise visibility with particular equipment. Clear deep-sky filters keeps the selected object, saved list, site and time.

Saved-place removal now offers Undo for the latest removal during the current visit. The selected observing site remains usable; Undo restores the place at its prior list position and preserves an already re-saved place’s current settings. Storage failures are reported as visit-only changes. The Undo snapshot ends at the next removal or reload.

Equipment removal offers Undo for the latest successfully removed setup while Photo tools remains open. Removal retains the working imaging draft. Undo preserves a setup re-saved under the same name, and failed writes keep the saved list unchanged. Keyboard focus moves to Undo after removal and returns to the saved-equipment heading after recovery.

Sky atlas now has top-of-tab shortcuts for camera discovery, the sky chart, deep-sky explorer and observing diary. Camera access is still explicit inside the viewer; closing returns focus to the button that opened it. The shortcuts and manual/fullscreen dialog were checked at desktop, 390 × 844 and 320 × 568 without horizontal page overflow. Hardware camera/motion accuracy remains part of phone acceptance.

Atlas shortcuts wait up to 15 seconds for a deferred section on a slow connection, then focus and scroll to it when ready. A loading failure leaves a retry message instead of an apparently inert link; leaving Sky atlas cancels the pending jump. Location-selection callbacks are stable, with explicit effect dependencies for browser location and the page tool. Lint now has no warnings.

Automated test files use isolated temporary Vite caches, cleaned after their servers close. This avoids parallel test workers regenerating the same default dependency cache or interfering with a running developer server. The complete 183-test suite passed three consecutive runs after isolating caches.

Both deep-sky and bright-star saved lists offer Undo for the latest removal until another target is removed or Sky atlas is left. Notes and diary records remain intact. Undo keeps a target that has already been re-saved, and returns focus to the saved-list heading. Restoring a removed target does not change the selected observing time or site.
