Saved places and equipment setups share the backup limits of 100 records each. At capacity, an existing place or setup can still be updated; adding a new one produces a clear message without changing the list. Undo also respects capacity. Oversized legacy lists are preserved for manual cleanup instead of being silently truncated. Browser checks with synthetic full lists confirmed addition rejection, setup replacement and Undo capacity handling; maximum-size collections remain exportable in regression tests.

Dark-sky discovery filter: catalogue-wide recommendations share 97 Sun samples and reuse calculations while search text changes. Eligibility requires altitude strictly above 30° and Sun altitude at or below −18°, sampled every 15 minutes over the selected 24-hour window. Results rank by qualifying peak and display local date/time with UTC offset alongside current altitude. This is an altitude/darkness opportunity, excluding weather, Moon glare, terrain and equipment. It composes with type, constellation, magnitude and current-altitude filters. Clear resets filters without changing the planner time, site, selection or saved lists. Tests compare recommendations against independent per-target windows, bound catalogue size and cover polar daylight and repeated local clock hours. Browser checks verified 38 qualifying London targets, correct descending order, a one-target combined filter, 21 targets when also requiring current altitude above 30°, and the polar-daylight empty state. Desktop, 390 × 844 and 320 × 568 layouts had no page overflow or console errors.

Catalogue name display: comma-separated common names now have a primary label and an explicit alias list. Compact results, saved lists, comparison headings and imaging selectors use the primary name; details retain the alternate names and search still matches them. Empty common names fall back to the catalogue identifier or Messier ID. A pure naming helper avoids pulling astronomy calculations into the imaging selector. Tests preserve source records and verify fallback, deduplication and alias search. Browser QA confirmed Cork Nebula and Little Dumbbell Nebula both find M76, comparison and imaging handoff show Barbell Nebula, and 320 × 568 detail text fits without page overflow or console errors. The temporary M76 favourite was removed and the original manual imaging centre restored.

Bright-target browsing: Unicode-compatible, case-insensitive and whitespace-normalized names now work in the 39-object finder. A separate Above 30° only control composes with search and the existing horizon filter. Clear target filters restores the full list without altering selection, site, time, notes or saved favourites. The shared pure search helper preserves source records and ordering; tests cover full-width/pasted names, combining accents, strict zero/30-degree boundaries and source retention. Browser QA confirmed a full-width spaced Vega query, four objects above 30° for the London test time, and Clear restoring all 39 while keeping Vega selected and the planner time unchanged. At 320 × 568 the controls and results fit without page overflow or console errors.

Observing-site metadata recovery: coordinate-only actions resolve missing country, time zone and Bortle fields from the matching saved site, then matching current context. Explicit values, including UTC, take precedence; metadata never carries between different coordinates. The location callback remains stable and reads current refs, avoiding location-session or page-tool re-registration on ordinary state updates. The WebMCP response returns the resolved site shown by the planner. Tests cover source preservation, explicit overrides, matching tolerance, unrelated sites and invalid input. Browser QA retained Europe/London and Bortle 4 after coordinate-only London selection, displayed 21:00 in site-local mode for the original 20:00 UTC instant, and returned those resolved fields from the tool. UTC display was restored and the console remained clean.

Storage-failure recovery: bright-target notes and target-list messages now identify the component lifetime instead of promising visit-wide retention after an atlas unmount. Camera preference failures identify the open viewer lifetime. Failed note writes show Retry saving notes, which reuses the current text and disappears after success. An isolated origin was filled to real browser quota using only a synthetic QA filler; note, list and reference-thumbnail preference writes failed recoverably. Removing the filler from a second QA tab allowed retry without leaving or retyping the form. The saved note survived reload; mobile warnings fit without page overflow and no console warnings/errors were observed. All QA-origin records, workers and caches were removed. An 800 × 400 landscape pass also checked new filters, camera fullscreen controls and scrollable settings; settings returns to the regular sky viewer, and closing restores the launch button focus. Live camera/motion accuracy remains a physical-phone acceptance test.

Deep-sky plan metadata: exported notes include the catalogue designation, full constellation name/code and alternate common names alongside J2000 coordinates, magnitude and major-axis size. Missing numeric fields remain explicit. The export test checks the helper and final printable HTML together. The real app prepared the three-target plan without console errors; the browser tool did not deliver a download event, so a captured browser-download file is not claimed. The temporary M76 favourite was removed. Native phone download/print handling remains a release check.

## 7 October 2026 — shared export lifecycle

All nine exports across eight components now request files through `lib/download.ts`. Requests remain synchronous within the user gesture, briefly attach a hidden anchor to the document, remove it after activation, and retain the Blob URL for 60 seconds before revocation. Failed attachment or activation releases the URL immediately and propagates to existing recovery messages. Meteor calendar and mosaic CSV exports now catch those failures too; meteor messaging no longer claims browser download completion.

199 automated tests, TypeScript, lint and the production build passed. Four new tests cover synchronous activation, file metadata, delayed cleanup, failure paths and retry. Built-app calendar preparation was exercised at desktop and 320 × 568 with no document overflow, console errors or leftover hidden anchors. The in-app download event timed out, so actual downloaded-file receipt and phone handling remain release checks.

## 7 October 2026 — responsive exports and atlas calculation reuse

A synthetic exported plan with a long unbroken site name overflowed a 305-pixel document to 1,580 pixels. Applying wrapping to the site heading returned the document width to 305 pixels at a 320 × 568 viewport. The checklist remained selectable and reference metadata readable. A generated diary retained all 2,000 note characters and long equipment/site text without horizontal overflow. Deep-sky plans now label generated notes and introductory guidance as catalogue references; ordinary target plans still identify personal notes.

The explorer computes the current 109-object catalogue positions once per observing time/place and reuses them for search/filter/saved-list rendering. Dark-window date formatting reuses one formatter per time zone. This avoids repeated current-position calculations on query changes; no measured phone speed-up is claimed.

200 automated tests, TypeScript, lint, production build and Cloudflare packaging dry-run passed. Built-app mobile checks confirmed Cork Nebula alias search, dark-window output, clearing back to 109 objects, and M31 altitude changing from 52.0° to 61.3° when the observing time changed by one hour, then returning correctly when restored. No browser console errors were reported. Saved records were retained through the app update. Native file receipt, printing/PDF and phone hardware remain pending.

## 7 October 2026 — weather feed validation and recovery

Browser parsing and the weather API now validate hourly timestamps and array alignment. Feeds require 1–240 unique increasing timestamps separated by at least an hour, with an overall span bounded to ten days; gaps remain explicit. Cloud coverage is required and supported optional fields must align to timestamps. Metric arrays are copied and restricted to supported fields. Invalid percentages, negative wind/visibility and non-finite samples become unavailable rather than plausible scores. Unknown/invalid time zones keep the existing fallback behavior. The API returns an uncached 502 on malformed provider data rather than caching a 200 response.

206 tests, TypeScript, lint, build and Cloudflare packaging passed. New tests cover clock-change samples, gaps, finite zero values, malformed timestamp/metric arrays, independent copies and provider error responses. A local synthetic weather fixture showed an initially valid forecast, then served malformed data: the built app retained its prior forecast with the existing warning and Retry control; a valid retry cleared the error. Checked at 390 × 844 without document overflow or console errors. Test-origin data, workers, caches and tabs were cleaned up; no user location or records were changed.

## 7 October 2026 — foreground PWA update polling

`watchPwaUpdateChecks` now schedules a 15-minute foreground check as well as return/reconnect checks. Hidden and offline apps do not request updates. The poll shares the existing cooldown and pending-request guard with lifecycle events; cleanup cancels the interval and removes event listeners. It offers available updates through the existing explicit reload flow rather than automatically reloading.

208 automated tests, TypeScript, lint, production build and Cloudflare packaging dry-run passed. Two new lifecycle tests cover a continuously open app, hidden/offline suppression, pending checks, event coalescing and disposal. The final built app loaded the real Open-Meteo forecast for the existing London test site without console errors, confirming compatibility with the new weather validation. No 15-minute installed-phone or production deployment update test is claimed. All owned preview servers and browser tabs were stopped/closed; changes remain local for GitHub Desktop.

## 7 October 2026 — preserve coordinate epochs in saved deep-sky exports

The new preview QA exposed a pre-existing export bug: Astronomy Engine's Horizon result includes apparent RA/declination as well as altitude/azimuth. Spreading that result over a catalogue object overwrote its J2000 coordinates, which the exported plan still labelled J2000. `deepSkyPosition` now returns only altitude and azimuth so adding horizontal positions cannot change catalogue coordinates. The actual horizontal calculations are unchanged. A regression check combines catalogue and position data at 2026 and 2100, then verifies original coordinates, exported J2000 text, survey URLs and source immutability. Previously downloaded deep-sky plans should be regenerated to correct their coordinate metadata.

## 7 October 2026 — in-app observing document previews

Saved bright-target plans, deep-sky plans and filtered diary reports can now be reviewed before downloading. A shared accessible dialog creates HTML only when opened, keeps a frozen snapshot and filename, and downloads that exact snapshot. Checklist marks are temporary and excluded from the download; diary previews describe saved records and exclude unfinished forms. The embedded generated document scrolls independently with close/download controls outside it. It uses a sandbox with scripts permitted for its Escape bridge but without same-origin, modal, popup or form permissions, following the [MDN iframe sandbox reference](https://developer.mozilla.org/en-US/docs/Web/HTML/Reference/Elements/iframe). A close message is accepted only from the current preview window. Downloaded HTML remains unchanged and includes its normal standalone print control.

211 automated tests, TypeScript, lint, production build and Cloudflare packaging passed. Browser checks covered all three preview types, 320/390-pixel portrait, 800 × 400 landscape, correct saved notes/equipment, filtered diary counts, current J2000 export coordinates, checklist interaction and Escape/focus restoration from inside the iframe. Download preparation showed a recoverable status and kept the preview open; no native file receipt or print success is claimed. Browser console checks reported no warnings/errors. Existing records and original filters were retained/restored.

## 7 October 2026 — diary removal with Undo

Saved diary entries can now be removed to free space within the shared 200-entry cap. Removal reads the latest validated diary, refuses a changed/missing version and updates the rendered list only after storage succeeds. Undo restores all original fields without overwriting a record that has been re-saved or duplicating its ID. It lasts until the next removal or leaving Sky atlas; backups and unfinished form/edit text remain unchanged. Recovery feedback is adjacent to the Undo action, with focus moved to Undo after removal and to the saved-list heading after restoration. Storage failures retain Undo and use readable recovery guidance.

216 tests, TypeScript, lint, production build and Cloudflare packaging passed. Five new tests cover original-field round trips, source immutability, concurrent changes, duplicate prevention, capacity and malformed data. On an isolated mobile origin, removing a record while editing preserved unfinished text; Undo restored its saved original. Filling real storage quota after removal made Undo fail safely; freeing the test pressure and retrying restored the complete record, including after reload. Original notes, Unicode, equipment, site and exact UTC were retained. Console checks were clean and the disposable origin data, workers, caches and tabs were removed.

## 7 October 2026 — consistent Moon phase and illumination

The eight phase-name ranges now handle new moon on both sides of the 360°/0° longitude wrap. `moonInfo` uses Astronomy Engine's illuminated fraction instead of approximating it with the cosine of ecliptic longitude difference; the deep-sky context reuses that fraction so it agrees with the Moon dashboard, forecasts and exports. Globe lighting remains an approximation and is labelled accordingly.

219 tests, TypeScript, lint, production build and Cloudflare packaging passed after fixing a missing import found by the new cross-section test. New tests cover all phase centres and boundaries, a real October 2026 new-moon crossing, and illumination consistency over 30 dates. The built mobile Moon page showed New Moon before the crossing without horizontal overflow or console errors. The original observing time was restored and owned browser/server resources were closed.

## 7 October 2026 — bright-star coordinate-frame consistency

Bright stars previously fed fixed J2000 coordinates directly to the date-of-observation Horizon calculation. They now share a J2000-to-date helper with the deep-sky catalogue; the bright-star pass computes its rotation once and reuses it for all stars. The helper returns only altitude/azimuth, preserving catalogue coordinates and J2000 survey requests. Star proper motion is still not modelled, and phone camera/compass accuracy remains unverified. The Galactic Centre region-direction implementation was not changed in this batch.

222 tests, TypeScript, lint, build and Cloudflare packaging passed. Cross-catalogue tests cover 34 stars at 2000/2026/2100, a measurable departure from the fixed-equator shortcut, unchanged source/photo coordinates and finite bounds at both poles. Browser checks confirmed Vega selection in the 3D atlas and manual camera centring on Polaris at 390 × 844, with a reference thumbnail, near-centre guidance and usable fullscreen controls. No camera, motion or location permission was requested. The original selected target/time were restored; browser logs were clear and owned browser/server resources were closed.

## 7 October 2026 — Unicode backup capacity

The old 1 MB file bound could reject a legal diary of 200 entries with 2,000-character Chinese notes. The parser and file chooser now share a 5 MB bound, and export validates its actual two-space-indented JSON. The diary reader's character bound was raised to 5 MiB so it can reread validated escaped-text records that saving permits. Country metadata now has the same 199-character bound as site names, keeping maximum validated metadata bounded. Record counts, note lengths, merge conflict rules and rollback behavior remain enforced. Browser storage quotas are separate and can still prevent writes.

225 tests, TypeScript, lint, production build and Cloudflare packaging passed. New tests cover full-capacity Unicode round trips, maximum escaped note/metadata serialization and reading back a maximum-length diary. A 1,273,735-byte synthetic backup reached the normal chooser/review flow at a 390-pixel mobile viewport, imported 200 observations, retained 2,000 Chinese characters per note after reload, and prepared a fresh backup without errors or overflow. Native downloaded-file receipt remains unverified. The file chooser took unexpectedly long; oversized-file rejection is covered by the shared parser/unit boundary rather than a second long chooser operation. Disposable origin data, workers, caches, tabs and servers were cleaned up.

## 7 October 2026 — imported note management and capacity recovery

A 100-note imported collection could previously grow beyond the backup-compatible count when a new bright-target note was entered. Saving now validates the shared 100-note/2,000-character/name constraints before writing. A note manager exposes imported and older catalogue names, with editing, removal and Undo for the latest removal while Sky atlas remains open. Restored text at a full store remains in the view with explicit retry/free-space guidance. Oversized valid legacy collections remain readable instead of silently dropping entries. Invalid raw storage remains unchanged when it cannot be read.

Pending note edits are held separately and overlaid on storage refreshes. Saving rereads the latest stored map before applying those edits, keeping unrelated cross-tab updates. This remains a local-storage read/modify/write operation rather than an atomic multi-tab transaction. Closing/reloading can lose unsaved edits as the UI explains. Removal moves focus to Undo; Undo returns focus to the restored textarea.

229 tests, TypeScript, lint, production build and Cloudflare packaging passed. Four new tests cover capacity, unchanged originals, latest-map merging, oversized legacy recovery, invalid data, Unicode and storage failures. A disposable browser origin with 100 archived notes retained a new Vega note at capacity and across another-tab updates, saved it after removing an old note, and retained it plus unrelated newer text after reload. Undo at capacity retained the original removed text; freeing a slot saved the restoration. 390/320-pixel layouts and editor-focus recovery were checked without overflow or console errors. Test data, workers, caches, tabs and servers were cleaned up.

## 7 October 2026 — explain initial compass setup

An initially upright Safari-style pose can have usable gyro/compass fields while the device-top horizontal projection has no stable north anchor. The existing tracker safely treated it as relative, but the UI only suggested aligning a known object. Readings now expose a `needsNorthAnchor` hint for this specific state. Regular and fullscreen camera views explain that names are paused and suggest tilting slightly away from upright to initialize the compass, with manual known-object alignment retained as an option. The tracker/projection math, north-anchor grace periods and hardware permission flow are unchanged.

231 tests, TypeScript, lint without warnings, production build and Cloudflare packaging passed. New synthetic-stream tests cover upright start, tilted anchoring, return upright, and absence of the cue for relative-only, unusable-compass and absolute streams. No physical phone behavior or rendered sensor state is claimed; those need device testing. An unused type import was removed after lint identified it.

## 7 October 2026 — valid mosaic coordinates after export rounding

Mosaic panel right ascension is normalized before export, but seven-decimal rounding could produce 24.0000000 hours. The CSV now wraps the rounded value to zero, retaining the same sky direction and seven-decimal precision without mutating the source panels. Two regression tests cover the rounding boundary and a rotated two-row grid crossing RA zero, including panel identities, declination precision and CRLF rows.

233 tests, TypeScript, lint without warnings, production build and Cloudflare packaging dry-run passed. This is a serialization correction; native file receipt remains pending.

## 7 October 2026 — dated Milky Way windows

The Milky Way planning card previously showed only the starting day beside an overnight time range. Both endpoints and the peak now show their dates, years and UTC offsets, resolving midnight and repeated-clock ambiguity. The label and explanation identify altitude as the ranking criterion and state the actual inclusive sampled Sun/core thresholds. The underlying calculations remain unchanged. NASA confirms the existing image coordinates but does not state their epoch on the linked page, so no coordinate-frame assumption was introduced for this regional marker: https://science.nasa.gov/asset/hubble/compass-and-scale-image-for-milky-way-center/

233 tests, TypeScript, lint without warnings, production build and Cloudflare packaging dry-run passed. The built planner displayed a public Sydney test window from 21 April to 22 April with GMT+10 at each endpoint; its peak carried the next-day date. The 320-pixel document had no horizontal overflow, and a 390-pixel screenshot was reviewed. Browser console warnings/errors were absent. Original London metadata and observing time were restored; the owned tab and preview server were closed.

A screenshot was reviewed and the disposable browser tab/server closed. No user data was changed.

Two calculation tests now check the overnight Sydney fixture, window bounds and contiguous qualifying samples, peak dominance across the full rolling 24 hours, and a polar-day fixture with no invented darkness. Comparison cards now retain a site’s last successful feed and timestamp during a refresh and after malformed/failed requests. My places offers an inline Rename site form using the existing shadcn Input/Button/Field components. Camera discovery now offers bright sky (39 original targets, the default), Messier deep sky (109) and combined (148 unique identities). The selected catalogue lasts until the viewer closes. ## 7 October 2026 — defined frame for the Milky Way sightline

The earlier NASA image-centre reference did not explicitly state an epoch. The regional marker now uses the IAU galactic longitude/latitude origin converted by Astronomy Engine to J2000, then the same date-frame horizontal transformation as catalogue objects. The marker is explicitly a Sagittarius-region sightline, not a point-source position for Sagittarius A*. Source/definition wording and the reference link were updated: https://github.com/cosinekitty/astronomy/blob/master/source/js/README.md#Rotation_GAL_EQJ. Installed library documentation and the primary source both define the GAL-to-EQJ transform.

The existing overnight-window and polar-day tests remain valid. On a disposable origin at 390 pixels, the Sydney 2D marker agreed with the card at 61.3°; using the peak time updated both to 85.1° and retained correct local dates/offsets. No overflow or console errors occurred. Test-origin storage, caches, workers, tabs and servers were cleaned.

The camera target picker now searches bright names and Messier catalogue names/aliases/numbers using the existing Unicode and spaced-text normalization. ## 7 October 2026 — preserve exact observing-plan instants

The observing plan’s UTC INSTANT field previously truncated seconds, including values carried from live camera positions or precise event times. It now preserves the full ISO instant (seconds and milliseconds), with seconds in the local date/time display. This changes presentation only, preserving the selected date and source coordinates. An existing deep-sky plan assertion was updated from the old minute-only display after the full suite identified it.

257 tests, TypeScript, lint without warnings, production build and Cloudflare packaging passed. A generated report at 320 pixels displayed 2026-12-31 23:59:59.987 UTC and 1 January local time without overflow; its checkbox worked and console checks were clear. ## 7 October 2026 — valid meteor dates and remaining peak windows

Meteor dates now use a full-year UTC helper, avoiding Date.UTC’s conversion of years below 100 to 1900–1999. Calendar exports reject nonexistent dates/unsupported year ranges before creating an event and fold all content lines within 75 UTF-8 octets. Common modern shower identifiers and all-day date semantics remain unchanged.

Upcoming-year selection uses the end of the typical peak night at site-local noon, instead of waiting until the following UTC midnight. Conditions exclude elapsed/partial hours before the selected observing instant and reject an hour extending beyond the local period’s end, including fractional-offset zones. ## 8 October 2026 — review conflicting retained note edits

Target-note edits capture their original version when first changed. Automatic save/retry merges only if the latest saved value still matches that base or already matches the intended edit. The version panels retain exact multiline Unicode text, are bounded vertically and expose labelled keyboard-focusable scroll regions. Review announcements avoid duplicating the selected-note live status while the review is open.

## 8 October 2026 — conditional page-exit protection

The root attaches its beforeunload listener synchronously only while the shared edit registry contains pending text, and removes it as soon as the final owner clears. Independent owners keep protection until all clear; cleanup unsubscribes and permits safe remounting. The handler retains preventDefault and uses a truthy legacy return value. This follows [Mozilla's beforeunload guidance](https://developer.mozilla.org/en-US/docs/Web/API/Window/beforeunload_event), which recommends conditional listeners because unconditional listeners affect Firefox history caching. Mobile browsers may suppress native prompts, so this is not a replacement for saving or recovery copies.

## 8 October 2026 — isolate offline files across waiting PWA releases

Production workers now derive their offline-cache name from the existing release fingerprint, with a v7 prefix. Offline HTML and the recovery module are read only from that worker's own cache, rather than a global cache lookup. Installing a waiting release cannot overwrite the active pair; even a failed second cache write leaves the active release untouched. Activation prunes obsolete Nightjar offline caches while preserving the shared bounded immutable-asset cache and unrelated caches. Identical build inputs retain the same cache name, while changes to worker/app/offline/recovery content change it. A failed install can leave an inactive partial cache until a later successful activation cleans it; it is never used by the active release.

## 8 October 2026 — aurora timing and polar cell accuracy

Provider/browser aurora validation now requires exact four-digit UTC calendar timestamps, with seconds and optional three-digit milliseconds. Dates repaired by Date.parse, zone-less values and forecasts preceding observations are rejected. Provider timestamps normalize before the API returns them. Freshness distinguishes invalid timing, old outlooks and timestamps unexpectedly ahead of the device clock. Nightjar uses a conservative two-hour future-forecast threshold and fifteen-minute observation/fetch-clock tolerance; these are application checks, not a new NOAA contract. Future-clock guidance and the heading ask users to check the device time instead of describing a future feed as an old outlook. Timestamp labels now include the year.

Nearest-cell selection uses haversine angular distance, accounting for converging longitude lines and the date line. Exact poles have deterministic ties; empty grids/invalid observing coordinates return no cell. The component memoizes that lookup by feed/site coordinates. The Aurora card now gives text a flexible desktop column and uses a single mobile column, avoiding a 100-pixel heading/description beside the percentage. A first CSS attempt adjusted flex properties, but browser computed styles showed that the later professional stylesheet uses a grid; the final grid-specific rule was verified after rebuilding.

350 tests, TypeScript, lint without warnings, production build, Cloudflare dry-run packaging and diff checks passed. Three additions cover exact calendar/UTC/chronology validation, leap days/milliseconds, freshness boundaries, malformed/future clocks, high-latitude/date-line/pole geometry and source immutability. Interoperability validation against the [current public NOAA feed](https://services.swpc.noaa.gov/json/ovation_aurora_latest.json) accepted 65,160 cells and normalized observation 2026-10-08T14:00:00.000Z / forecast 2026-10-08T15:15:00.000Z with no timing issue. This verifies that sample, not all future provider formats.

No push or deployment.

## 9 October 2026 — guarded import snapshots and attempted-write rollback

Backup import now derives its merge/review and rollback values from the same bounded raw snapshot, then checks each stored key before replacement. A detected peer change rolls back this import's matching writes and exposes a fresh review while preserving the peer value. Local storage is not transactional: these checks narrow interleaving windows, not guarantee cross-process atomicity.

Its explanatory text accurately describes the existing algorithm: each night prefers a complete two-hour pair, uses a single hour when no pair exists, then the best upcoming nightly choice is ranked by score with duration breaking ties. The previous text implied every individual one/two-hour interval was ranked solely by score. The algorithm itself is unchanged. Three regression groups use actual sky calculations to protect this policy, global ties and refusal to invent pairs over gaps, missing coverage or already-started hours.

All 449 tests, types, lint, production build and Cloudflare dry-run packaging passed. A two-site synthetic feed on port 5353 supplied a cloudy pair and a disconnected clearer hour. The current selected hour scored 100 while the preferred nightly pair scored 28; the new explanation matched that distinction. Both cards retained their source conditions and showed the complete 21 January 2036 20:00–22:00 local interval. Plan this window selected exactly 20:00 UTC. Native desktop and 320-pixel screenshots showed the comparison and wrapped interval; compact cards matched client/scroll widths at 233 pixels and the page at 305 pixels. No captured console warnings/errors. Screenshots inspected/emitted, no files saved. Test storage/workers/caches/tab, viewport and owned servers cleaned. Local commit only, no push/deployment.

## 11 October 2026 — suspend PWA update polling when unavailable

The service-worker update watcher previously kept a fifteen-minute interval while hidden/offline and merely skipped requests. It now cancels polling on visibility loss, offline and pagehide, starts one timer on usable return, and checks immediately subject to the existing one-minute cooldown and in-flight guard. A generation check ignores queued callbacks from cancelled timers. Pagehide suspends even before visibility changes; pageshow restores availability. No automatic activation or reload was added.

All 478 automated tests passed, including three lifecycle regressions covering hidden/offline startup, cancellation/resumption, stale callbacks and pagehide with still-visible state. Fourteen targeted clock/update checks, TypeScript, lint, production build and Cloudflare packaging dry-run passed. The bundled runtime no longer exposes npm; equivalent package-script executables ran directly without dependency changes.

Browser plugin was unavailable; CUA-supported Playwright QA used the actual production build on disposable proxy origin http://127.0.0.1:5421. Public fixture controls simulated lifecycle/connectivity and counted the real watcher timer and registration.update calls. Initial/hide/return timer counts were 1/0/1, with one update on return. Offline/online and pagehide/pageshow each produced 0/1 timers and one fresh update after advancing the cooldown. An unfinished empty observing-time entry remained protected; direct review focused observing-time with its value unchanged and the original 20:00 UTC selected. At 320×568, page client/scroll widths both measured 305 pixels. Page identity, meaningful content, absence of framework overlays and console health passed. Native screenshot inspected/emitted; no file saved. Synthetic events do not establish physical OS suspension or measured battery improvement. Local commit only; no push/deployment.

## 11 October 2026 — keep image-loading recovery tied to foreground visibility

Reference photos and lunar textures share a visibility-aware loading deadline. Backgrounding now cancels that deadline; return starts a fresh fifteen seconds only if the frame remains in the viewport. Hidden/offscreen starts schedule no warning. A generation token ignores queued callbacks from cancelled timers, preventing an old scroll-away deadline from firing early or replacing the next countdown. The fallback without IntersectionObserver remains usable; cleanup removes the visibility listener and pending deadline.

## 11 October 2026 — bound every deep-sky planning sample

Individual deep-sky windows, shared catalogue recommendations and saved-target comparisons now use one supported fifteen-minute sample generator. ## 12 October 2026 — retain useful GitHub verification evidence

The validation workflow now tees npm run verify output into a runner-temporary log with pipefail, preserving failure status. An always-run summary identifies the validation step outcome, available TAP totals and up to 50 failed-test/summary lines. Missing verification output points to earlier setup/install steps; a passing test count explicitly does not establish later build/packaging success. The workflow attempts a seven-day, commit-named log artifact. Artifact retention is nonblocking and does not turn successful validation into an application failure if upload is unavailable; an actual failed validation remains failed. Existing trigger, runner, timeout, Node setup and read-only repository permissions are unchanged.

The YAML parsed with the installed js-yaml dependency. Extracted Bash blocks ran locally against controlled test failure (exit 7), later build failure after passing totals (exit 9), success, 80 failed lines and skipped/missing-log cases. Nonzero validation statuses survived tee, original output was retained, summary truncation did not cause pipe errors and missing logs were explicit. The actual extracted capture command passed Node 22.16.0 full verification: all 540 tests, TypeScript, warning-free lint, production build and Cloudflare packaging dry-run. Its summary correctly showed success and 540/540/0 test totals. No new application tests, dependencies or runtime changes were needed.

GitHub's official [artifact documentation](https://docs.github.com/en/actions/tutorials/store-and-share-data) and [job-summary reference](https://docs.github.com/en/actions/reference/workflows-and-actions/workflow-commands#adding-a-job-summary) support the configured artifact and summary mechanisms. These were read-only references. No hosted upload, runner execution, remote retry, push or deployment was performed; hosted evidence for this workflow remains a post-push gate. ## 13 October — resample offline-page connection hints on foreground return

The offline page previously listened only to online/offline events, leaving its hint stale when a mobile return missed those events. It now samples connectivity on pageshow and visible return, deferring visibility callbacks during pagehide suspension. Returning from suspension offers a manual Refresh saved details action and explains that data may have changed; it does not claim a known change, rewrite stored plans or automatically replace the displayed snapshot. A later relevant storage event supplies the existing definite changed-in-another-view message.

A failed site/time-preservation message also now survives connection/return events. Only a successful explicit retry clears that message, so network sampling cannot hide the reason navigation was prevented. Four new regressions failed before repair (19 existing offline tests passed). Full Node 22 verification passed afterward with 575 tests, TypeScript, lint, build and Cloudflare packaging dry-run. All 575 tests also pass under Node 24.19.

Production integration at 320×568 used the actual generated worker, a disposable root-503 gateway, synthetic connectivity/visibility flags and synthetic session-write failure controls. Native worker control was observed before reloading through the outage; the cached offline page displayed London QA, Polaris, its exact multiline Unicode note and 2026-10-25T01:30:17.456Z. Silent online restoration plus a queued visible event did not refresh the suspended hint; pageshow updated it and offered manual snapshot refresh, retaining all text/time. A blocked retry kept the preservation failure through another silent disconnect/return. After allowing writes and restoring the planner, explicit Try again online performed real navigation and retained site, saved Polaris, the exact note and all seconds/milliseconds. Document width was 305/305 in the offline fixture and final warning/error logs were empty. Screenshots were inspected. The fixture toolbar adds space and uses simulated networking; this does not establish native phone connectivity or installed-PWA lifecycle.

## 13 October — cancel pending offline recovery preparation

The offline recovery download now offers Cancel preparing recovery copy while its module is pending. Cancellation invalidates the request, keeps stored plans intact and returns focus to the download button. Late module completion/failure cannot start a cancelled download, overwrite feedback or enable a newer pending request. Current-request completion hides Cancel and returns focus if that control held it.

Three new async regressions failed before repair, including obsolete completion and obsolete failure while a newer request was pending. After the delayed module arrived, an explicit retry prepared exactly one copy through a counter that forwards native anchor clicks. Polaris’s multiline Unicode note and 2026-10-25T01:30:17.456Z remained displayed; document width stayed 305/305 and warning/error logs were empty. Screenshots were inspected. This verifies download-request behaviour, not native file receipt or importability of raw recovery copies.

## 13 October — recheck earlier import writes before reporting success

Backup import previously guarded only the collection about to be written. A peer replacement of an earlier written collection could therefore survive while the import returned a stale success result. A regression covering replacement after the first and final writes failed on the previous source. The transaction now checks every collection against either its original or locally written snapshot before each write and once before success. Existing rollback refuses to overwrite newer peer values; the error distinguishes changed plans from a storage failure. This is guarded verification, not an atomic multi-key storage transaction.

## Readable full-capacity diary-form persistence — 14 October 2026

## Deferred menu and viewer focus ownership — 14 October 2026

A paused animation-frame test reproduced mobile navigation from Tonight to Photo tools moving focus from a newly edited 700 mm focal-length field to the page heading. A shared deferred handoff now captures the originating action, resolves the destination after cleanup/render, and focuses/scrolls only while the action still owns focus or its disappearing control has left BODY focused. Hidden pages, disconnected destinations and modal-hidden ancestors refuse the handoff. Mobile navigation, expanded 2D details, expanded 3D details, ordinary chart details and camera details use it. The originating button is captured before dialog cleanup; late cleanup therefore cannot adopt a newly active editor as its owner.

Five regression scenarios cover ordinary/closed-owner navigation, preserving a different field or a hidden page, removed/hidden/missing destinations, post-render destination resolution and an explicit originating owner through late cleanup. Final Node 22 verification passed types, lint, all 605 tests, build and Cloudflare packaging. All five helper tests passed on Node 24.19. Typecheck caught the ordinary details button still passing a click event into the revised Element-owner signature; its callback now passes currentTarget explicitly before the final successful verification.

Browser plugin not available: CUA-provided Playwright checked a disposable production preview at http://127.0.0.1:5653. At 320×800, delayed menu navigation preserved the active 700 mm field. Ordinary navigation focused planner-heading and retained 2026-10-14T20:00 UTC. A blank time draft remained across section navigation, as intended; section changes do not discard the global time field. Camera-to-Polaris live details then queued its context change behind that draft, closed the camera, and focused unsaved-view-heading while preserving the blank draft and selected UTC instant. Keep editing and Cancel time edit restored the previous time. At 1024×800, expanded 2D and 3D details buttons closed their dialogs, focused target-details and retained Polaris/time. The 3D handoff was checked while its renderer initialization was deferred, rather than claiming a complete rendered-dome check. Meaningful app content, the field-focus screenshot, no framework overlay and empty warning/error logs supported the run. Native camera/motion, OS suspension, installed-PWA behavior and screen readers remain untested here.


## Full-screen discovery catalogue and measured scroll guidance — 14 October 2026

Full-screen camera now exposes the same three catalogue modes as Settings. A retained M31 query in Stars/solar-system mode previously reported zero results without a visible catalogue control; switching to Combined now offers M31 without leaving full screen. Mode changes use the existing selection-reset behavior and do not request camera/motion hardware. Messier/Combined modes retain the explanation that names are predicted directions and most objects need optics.

The phone review also found a 156-pixel chooser clipping its mode text, plus a 359-pixel control viewport with 506 pixels of content while scroll guidance was hidden. The chooser now stacks to full width below 500 pixels. An observer measures the full-screen control body against the panel's available content height; sticky guidance appears only when that body overflows. The observer is disconnected on exit and ignores queued callbacks for disconnected controls. The scroll body preserves the existing action/search layouts. A final CSS-only top offset covers the panel's eight-pixel padding strip while scrolling.

Full Node 22 verification passed types, lint, all 605 tests, build and Cloudflare packaging. The final CSS-only strip adjustment passed build/packaging and rendered checks. ## Final package reconstruction — 14 October 2026

The 90-input release reconstruction reproduced the complete generated worker at `0a617585d1df9ef32e1a1cb0e4cadac3dbdfc29fcfe1565244b7723636feefca`. ## Verify final site-name and sky-rating snapshots — 14 October 2026

Both save paths guarded their original reads and intermediate writes but returned success immediately after writing the selected site. A final-write peer replacement therefore passed unnoticed. New checks verify the selected site and saved list after that write. On failure, existing guarded rollback restores only values still matching this attempt; newer peer data remains untouched. Names stay in the editor, and failed rating changes leave the displayed rating unchanged. These checks detect observed replacement; they do not make localStorage transactional or promise protection against a later write after validation.

## Cancellable backup reads and feedback focus — 14 October 2026

A deterministic File.text delay reproduced a disabled backup picker with no cancellation action. When that read completed after editing a site name, the import review unconditionally moved focus away from the editor. The backup panel now offers Cancel reading backup, invalidates obsolete generations on cancel/unmount, and returns to the picker after it is enabled again using the shared ownership guard. A cancelled read returns before parsing or reading current plans. File.text itself is not abortable here; its late result is ignored. Review/error focus is allowed only while its originating action still owns focus or BODY is active, and never while hidden or modal-hidden. Synchronous download/import feedback retains normal focus behavior. Import still requires explicit review and confirmation.

## Read current red-light preferences safely — 14 October 2026

The preference listener replayed StorageEvent.newValue and accessed localStorage without a guard. A queued older “off” event reproduced switching the page off while the stored preference was already on. Blocking the storage getter before delivering an event reproduced an uncaught SecurityError. The listener now reads the current matched storage area's value through a guarded helper; unrelated keys/areas and unavailable storage produce no display update. A successful stored read clears obsolete local save-error feedback. This does not add automatic preference writes or change the same-page toggle event.

Three regressions cover older preference/clear-event payloads, denied getters/reads and unrelated keys/areas. ## 15 October 2026 — polar map bounds and visible edge markers

The supported coordinate entry accepted latitude 90, longitude 180, but My places sent OpenStreetMap a bounding box reaching latitude 90.08. The embedded provider threw “Invalid LngLat latitude value: must be between -90 and 90” and showed a misleading world overview without its normal marker/controls. This was observed through the actual embedded map, not inferred from URL arithmetic. OpenStreetMap’s [projection documentation](https://osmdata.openstreetmap.de/info/projections.html) explains that its tiled Mercator view excludes the polar regions.

The new place-map helper leaves the observing coordinates untouched. It offers an explanation outside the usable map range (including a small margin for reliable marker framing at the projection edge), and shrinks latitude and longitude bounds together near that range. Ordinary map URLs stay identical; unwrapped longitude bounds preserve a small view across the date line. Astronomy, saved sites and time-zone selection keep their existing coordinate rules.

Browser plugin not available; CUA-provided Playwright tested the actual provider iframe through a disposable localhost production preview. At 320×800 and 1024×800, both poles showed the explanation without a failing iframe, and site editing remained available. A southern-pole site could still receive Bortle 2 and be saved. The final northern-pole check retained UTC 2026-10-15T20:00, used width 305/305 and had no warning/error logs.

The first bounded-map attempt exposed a further rendering issue at 85.05°, 180°: its 40-pixel pin began at y=-37.62. Shrinking only latitude still left it clipped at y=-30.47 because the provider’s world-edge constraint interacts with the wide requested view. Shrinking both axes produced a fully visible pin (y=36.23–76.23 in the 280-pixel compact map, y=36–76 on desktop). The southern 85.05°/−180° pin was also visible (y=163.77–203.77); its screenshot shows the complete green pin. A 51.5°/0° map kept its original URL and a complete pin at y=100.08–140.08. All final iframe checks had no warning/error logs. Exact marker coordinates were preserved.

Three regressions cover unavailable polar views, centred bounded edge requests with an unchanged marker, date-line spans, invalid coordinates and ordinary URL compatibility. The strengthened centring/edge tests failed the first partial adjustment; an ordinary-URL assertion also caught multiplication-order floating-point text drift, which was corrected. Full final Node 22 npm run verify passes 618 tests, types, lint, build and Cloudflare packaging dry-run. All 618 final tests also pass under Node 24.19. External provider availability and physical-phone acceptance remain separate gates.

## PWA update deadline after background return — 16 October 2026

Source review found applyPwaUpdate using an unconditional 15-second wall-clock timeout while other loading flows already respected foreground availability. A suspended app could therefore report an update failure before it had a fresh chance to process activation on return. It now reuses watchForegroundDelay in browsers, with injected lifecycle targets for tests and the existing ordinary timer fallback in non-browser callers. Disposal/activation/failure cancel deadline listeners together; existing preservation and unfinished-edit guards remain unchanged.

Two regressions fail against the prior implementation and pass after the fix: hidden visibility and pagehide without a visibility event both pause expiry; return permits activation or one genuine recovery timeout, and late controller events cannot reload after failure. ## Share concurrent Aurora provider reads — 16 October 2026

Route review found the completed-result cache did not cover in-flight reads: two simultaneous requests to an empty/expired Aurora cache each contacted NOAA. Three new route-level regressions reproduce duplicate calls against the original implementation (all three fail with 2 versus expected 1, /tmp/nightjar-aurora-coalescing-baseline.log). The route now owns one in-flight parsed-feed promise per Worker instance. Each caller constructs its own Response; success updates the existing five-minute cache, failure retains the same 502 guidance, and identity-checked finally cleanup allows future retries without clearing a newer request. The 15-second timeout, endpoint, response schema and cache headers are unchanged. This is instance-local coalescing, not a distributed cache or rate-limit guarantee.

Regressions cover simultaneous independent readable responses, exact cache expiry at five minutes, shared network rejection followed by successful retry/cache reuse, and HTTP failure, non-JSON bodies and invalid timestamp order remaining uncached and retryable. Full Node 22 verify passes types, lint, all 637 tests, production build and Cloudflare dry-run (/tmp/nightjar-aurora-coalescing-verify.log). All 637 tests also pass under Node 24.19 (/tmp/nightjar-aurora-coalescing-node24.log); focused Aurora tests pass separately (/tmp/nightjar-aurora-coalescing-focused.log).

Two HTTP requests released together through a Python barrier hit the actual compiled local Wrangler Worker at 5693 without a provider stub. Both returned 200, public max-age=300, 65,160 cells and the same fetchedAt 2026-10-16T07:44:08.670Z. Raw received responses are /tmp/nightjar-aurora-worker-1.json and /tmp/nightjar-aurora-worker-2.json. There was no cross-request I/O/runtime error in the Worker log. Matching timestamps supplement the deterministic provider-call-count tests; they do not independently measure upstream request counts or establish deployed multi-instance behavior. No UI or hardware flow changed, and no native/browser acceptance claim was added.

## Verify backup source before offering an export — 16 October 2026

Backup export previously read six saved collections once and immediately serialized them. A controlled replacement after the final initial read could leave a stale earlier collection in a successful exported file. makePlannerBackup now compares the raw source values once more before serialization/download; a changed value refuses the file with fresh-retry guidance, and denied final reads give storage-recovery guidance. Export never writes stored plans. This narrows a confirmed read race; localStorage still has no atomic multi-key snapshot and a later change cannot be ruled out by a finite verification pass. Import validation/merge/rollback behaviour is unchanged.

## Short polar altitude crossings between full-hour samples — 16 October 2026

Confirmed a calculation defect in hourBelowAltitude, used by the seven-night outlook and meteor conditions. All five quarter-hour samples for 21 December 2026, 12:00–13:00 UTC at latitude 85.07938514866692, longitude -2.4 are below -18° in Nightjar’s current apparent-altitude model. The Sun nevertheless rises above that threshold from approximately 12:04:19.952 to 12:10:57.471 UTC (6.625 minutes), peaking at about -17.9995°. The old helper returns true and permits the clear forecast hour as a complete dark window. The new integration regression fails before the fix and refuses that recommendation afterward.

Retained the fast five-sample rejection and added Astronomy Engine’s ascent search over the hour. The library expects an unrefracted threshold, so InverseRefraction removes the existing normal-refraction adjustment before searching; this preserves the app’s current altitude convention rather than silently changing twilight/scoring definitions. Library source documents its search subdivision for brief polar rise/set pairs and its finite event-time precision; this is not a promise of exact physical visibility or sub-second event detection. Invalid thresholds return false. A contour above the apparent zenith maximum returns true without invoking an inverse outside the model’s range; this also avoids the library’s unbounded inverse iteration at 90°.

A second real-engine fixture catches a brief Moon ascent at latitude 62.123953956451714, longitude -53.656755201860676 on 16 October 2026, 20:00–21:00 UTC: all five old samples are below zero, but the intervening centre altitude becomes positive. Nearby ordinary hours remain available. Tests also cover invalid/near-zenith contours, invalid dates, final-year range and unchanged site metadata. Targeted 18 tests pass. All 656 tests pass on Node 22.16.0 and Node 24.19.0; full Node 22 verify passes types, lint, build and actual Cloudflare packaging dry-run (114 staged assets, no bindings). Temporary logs: /tmp/nightjar-polar-dark-hour-before.log, /tmp/nightjar-dark-hour-targeted.log, /tmp/nightjar-dark-hour-verify.log and /tmp/nightjar-dark-hour-node24.log. ## Consistent geometric solar twilight across planners — 16 October 2026

Reproduced an inter-view contradiction at London (51.5°, 0°) on 16 October 2026. The exact “Full darkness begins” milestone is 18:55:38.863 UTC. One second later, the milestone/camera geometric Sun altitude is -18.00257026217001° and camera lighting says Astronomical darkness, but the deep-sky sample used normal refraction and reported -17.479512477656257°, rejecting darkness. USNO’s primary definitions specify the geometric Sun centre at -6°/-12°/-18°: https://aa.usno.navy.mil/faq/RST_defs. The installed Astronomy Engine SearchAltitude documentation likewise explicitly excludes atmospheric refraction for twilight.

Added shared sunAltitude and an explicit normal/none option on bodyPosition/hourBelowAltitude. Solar classifications, score daylight terms, first-open shortcuts, Milky Way/deep-sky/planet sampling, seven-night/meteor dark hours, selected-time dashboard/site comparisons, aurora Sun metadata and printable-plan Sun metadata now use the same geometric convention. Camera lighting delegates to that helper. Apparent Sun/Moon/star viewing, projection, Moon horizon checks, sunrise/sunset limb searches, eclipse positions and phase calculations retain their original model. The meteor explanatory copy and README distinguish geometric darkness from apparent Moon-centre horizon checks.

This deliberately supersedes the previous batch’s preservation of the apparent solar darkness contour: the continuous full-hour crossing protection is retained in both modes, while the planning callers now request the correct geometric solar contour. The previous apparent short-Sun-excursion fixture remains covered separately; the dark-window integration fixture uses a corresponding brief geometric excursion at latitude 84.56007072450302°, longitude -2.4° with the peak between quarter-hour samples.

Before the fix, three of the initial four cross-module regression tests fail: milestone/camera versus target-planner Sun values, a nonzero clear-sky score just after geometric civil dusk, and a complete geometric polar dark hour rejected by apparent refraction. Added first-open coverage retaining the next geometrically dark hour instead of skipping it, plus explicit default apparent viewing-position preservation. ## Aurora HTTP cache lifetime — 18 October 2026

Confirmed a server/cache-contract defect: the route's in-memory provider snapshot expires after five minutes, but a hit at 299,999 ms still returned public, max-age=300. A browser could reuse that nearly expired snapshot for almost another five minutes. Extended the existing route test to inspect response headers at 120,001 ms and 299,999 ms; it failed before the fix (300 rather than 179 seconds at the first checkpoint, /tmp/nightjar-aurora-cache-before.log). The intended interpretation of max-age follows [RFC 9111 section 5.2.2.1](https://www.rfc-editor.org/rfc/rfc9111.html#section-5.2.2.1).

The shared load now retains its own snapshot and expiry together. Both newly loaded and memory-cached responses derive a nonnegative whole-second max-age from that entry's remaining lifetime, capped at 300. The last fractional second advertises zero rather than extending expiry. Provider data, timestamps, five-minute server reuse, concurrent-request coalescing and failed-request retry behavior remain the same. The tests verify 179 seconds mid-life, zero at the last millisecond, and a new snapshot/300 seconds at the refresh boundary.

All 15 aurora tests, TypeScript, lint, production build and Cloudflare dry-run pass on Node 24.19.0 (/tmp/nightjar-aurora-cache-check.log). The dry-run stages 103 assets without bindings. Isolated artifact audit passes: 05174aaa8fae202f4b4f7b0367532d22033d210b0015e2087b6a112b78cc3be8, 81 emitted/recovery inputs, 312 relative references, 95 client files, manifest/icons and recovery bytes pass; zero generated copies omitted in this audit snapshot (/tmp/nightjar-aurora-cache-release.log). The prior full 662-test run predates this server fix; it was not repeated for the isolated route change. There is no rendered frontend change in this batch.

The watched Worker on 5705 reloaded between real HTTP requests, clearing its memory cache; its cache-hit assertion was inconclusive, not a reproduced fix failure. Stopped it and ran an isolated staged copy on 5706. Real NOAA responses both returned 200 and 65,160 cells, the same fetchedAt 2026-10-18T09:08:17.331Z and forecastAt 2026-10-18T09:53:00.000Z, while Cache-Control decreased from public, max-age=299 to public, max-age=288. The isolated log has two requests and no intervening reload (/tmp/nightjar-aurora-cache-isolated-worker.log). This validates local compiled-route headers, not production edge behavior or scientific model accuracy. Both workers stopped; the wrapper cleans its disposable staged directory. No push or deployment.

If saveDiary writes an updated observation and clearing the stored edit then fails, reopening restores that edit with its older original snapshot. The next Save diary changes reports that the observation changed, even though the saved entry exactly matches the recovered draft. Added a regression test for that persisted state; it failed before the fix (/tmp/nightjar-completed-edit-before.log).

The recovery filter now clears a changed edit from the recovered view only when the saved record with its ID exactly matches the draft. An unchanged explicitly opened edit still resumes, and missing records or newer/different peer data retain the draft. Tests cover changed notes/outcome, unrelated unfinished forms, a missing entry, the old saved version, newer notes/site metadata, unchanged edits and source immutability. This filter writes neither saved observations nor stored draft data; subsequent explicit form actions retain the existing merge guards.

Rendered before/after acceptance used a 390 × 844 frame at 127.0.0.1:5708/__qa, proxying isolated compiled Workers on 5706. Visible fixture controls seeded the valid stored result of a completed save plus failed edit cleanup, rather than intercepting storage APIs or simulating an actual quota failure. Before the fix, the completed M31 edit reopened, displayed its saved updated text, and Save diary changes produced the false changed-since-editing warning. After rebuilding, the same state showed a blank new-observation form and Save observation, while the saved M31 entry retained “Completed saved edit QA.” A second fixture case with newer peer-saved text kept both the recovered draft and the newer saved entry, and Save diary changes still correctly refused to overwrite it. Final completed-case rerun again removed the stale edit from the view. Screenshots were emitted of the old false conflict and fixed blank form; exact saved-entry claims rely on fresh DOM snapshots. Title is correct, meaningful content/no framework overlay, HTML client/scroll widths 375/375, and the inspected warning/error log is empty.

The full npm run verify command passes on pinned Node 22.16.0: TypeScript, lint, all 663 tests (no failures/cancellations/skips), build and Cloudflare dry-run (/tmp/nightjar-completed-edit-verify.log), staging 103 assets without bindings. Independent artifact audit passes for 688c325a1b04ffeae108578ca14df5bbdf9e93ada29c31a5b62e99800846baff: 81 inputs, 312 relative references, 95 client files, manifest/icons and recovery bytes match; zero generated copies omitted in that audit snapshot (/tmp/nightjar-completed-edit-release.log). Existing dependencies were reused on macOS; this is not a fresh hosted Linux install.

Fixture cleanup confirmed clearing its storage, workers and caches. Closed the owned tab and stopped the proxy and both before/after Workers; staging cleanup belongs to the wrapper. No push, deployment or device permission request.

## Date-line site identity — 18 October 2026

After the user's new continue request, confirmed a location-identity defect: samePlaceCoordinates used the direct longitude difference, so accepted +180° and −180° values identified separate saved sites. Four new regressions failed before the fix, with 47 of 51 focused tests passing (/tmp/nightjar-dateline-before.log). Consequences included missing saved metadata, duplicate saved entries and backup merges failing to recognize an existing site.

The shared matcher now accepts the existing <0.0001° longitude tolerance across the ±180° seam as well as ordinary longitude differences. It retains the entered coordinates and requested name rather than normalizing stored values. Tests cover both directions, nearby seam points, outside-tolerance/different-latitude/distant sites, saved country/time-zone/Bortle recovery, ambiguous named aliases, saving, Undo, backup conflict preview and input immutability. Existing records are not migrated or deleted.

Rendered before/after acceptance used an isolated compiled Worker on 5710 and a disposable fixture at 127.0.0.1:5711/__qa with a 390 × 844 frame. The fixture supplied only synthetic weather responses with a known time zone; it did not patch the app HTML or storage APIs. In the original build, entering −17.75°, −180° through the normal coordinate form lost country/Bortle metadata. My places showed Not rated and Save this place; Save then created a second stored record. The fixture's visible inspection control read the selected and saved records from storage to confirm those states.

In the rebuilt app, the same entry preserves country QA country and Bortle 3, with the entered −180° retained. Saved on this device acknowledges the existing location and leaves one saved record at its original +180° representation. The selected UTC instant remains 2026-10-18T19:00:00.000Z. Fresh DOM/storage-inspection snapshots confirm the result; a screenshot shows Bortle class 3 and Saved on this device. Correct title/meaningful content, no framework overlay, HTML client/scroll widths 375/375, and no warnings/errors in the inspected log. A pre-fix embedded-frame menu click reported No node found despite a visible match; fresh state and keyboard Enter restored navigation. This driver issue did not motivate the code change. No phone or hardware acceptance is claimed.

The complete npm run verify command passes on pinned Node 22.16.0 for runtime commit d3f0956: TypeScript, lint, all 667 tests (no failures/cancellations/skips), build and Cloudflare packaging dry-run (/tmp/nightjar-dateline-verify.log), staging 103 assets without bindings. ## Short-landscape photo/framing sidebar overlap — 18 October 2026

Confirmed a rendered layout defect in the shared enlarged-viewer CSS. At an 800 × 300 child viewport, the framing zoom controls occupied y=95–198 and the caption y=163–263, overlapping by 35 px and visibly painting caption text over Reset framing zoom. The short-landscape rule assigned both sections to the same grid cell, aligned at opposite ends. Changed it to separate rows, with the main image/drawing spanning both rows and a scrollable caption track. No image, framing, export or zoom calculation changed; no implementation-mirroring CSS unit test was added.

The first adjustment used an 80 px caption minimum. It removed the overlap but created 25 px of whole-dialog scrolling at 800 × 300, clipping the heading after caption focus. Focusing the photo's Image source link scrolls its caption to 162 px; the link's y=218.3–252.5 bounds fit inside y=208–263. No external source navigation was triggered. M31's featured NASA reference actually decodes at 800 × 800. Escape returns focus to each viewer's opener. The initial M31 enlargement lookup targeted a closed Reference image disclosure; opening that normal disclosure exposed the button and resolved the driver timeout. It did not motivate a product change. Screenshots were emitted before and after the layout fix. The inspected browser warning/error log is empty; correct title/meaningful content and no framework overlay. These are browser-frame checks, not physical-phone acceptance.

## Equipment framing on the sky atlas — 18 October 2026

Implemented the planned Photo tools → atlas connection in a53ef5b. Sky atlas can show the current imaging draft's camera/mosaic on both 2D and 3D views, including expanded dialogs. The visit-only toggle is owned by the planner so it survives section changes; Edit in Photo tools uses the existing navigation guard. Loading a saved equipment setup and editing grid fields feed the same draft back to the atlas. The 3D Centre imaging grid action preserves the selected atlas object, time and place.

Extracted imagingPlan so PhotoPlanner and the overlay share decimal parsing, equipment validation, field-of-view/image-scale calculation, mosaic limits and error messages. Projection uses the existing guide's J2000 tangent plane and east-of-north rotation, with eight samples per panel edge. Panel identities/centres agree with mosaic CSV order. The current catalogue id is shown only if its coordinates still match; otherwise the centre is identified as custom J2000. Each frame remains ≤10°, centre declination ±75°, 1–6 rows/columns, overlap 0–50%, rotation ±360°. Wide-grid orientations remain approximate away from the tangent-plane centre.

The 2D outline clips to its circular horizon. The dome batches all clipped edge segments into one LineSegments object rather than one draw object per segment, discards below-horizon portions and clips crossings at y=0. A separate centre marker is shown only above the horizon; grid-centring is disabled when its centre is below. Added meaningful geometry tests for an independent 4° × 3° equatorial frame, 90° rotation, RA zero, large rotated grids near +75° declination, shared precessed apparent positions at different sites/times, raw-field refusal and actual horizon-straddling geometry. Component rendering tests require an enabled valid draft and forbid drawing corrupt fallback defaults or incomplete coordinates.

Browser QA used an isolated compiled Worker on 5712 and a disposable control fixture on 5713. Its visible buttons seeded synthetic London QA, exact 2026-10-18T19:00Z, M31's current catalogue coordinates, two saved equipment profiles and either valid, malformed or horizon drafts. The fixture did not alter the app HTML or sensor/storage APIs; no hardware permissions were requested. At 390 × 844, the default 2 × 2 M31 grid shows 5.15° × 3.44° frames with centre altitude 49.4°. Centring it renders a visible cyan grid/label. Selecting and centring Vega, then centring the imaging grid, keeps Vega selected and moves only the dome view.

The 2D chart contains four finite sampled outlines under a uniquely named horizon clip. Its expanded mobile dialog has client/scroll widths 388/388. Loading QA narrow setup in Photo tools changes focal length to 1000 mm; editing rotation to 45° yields 1.26° × 48.13′ frames on return, while Vega and exact UTC time remain. A native embedded-frame menu click once reported No node found despite a visible enabled match; fresh state and keyboard Enter restored navigation, with no app-source change for that driver failure.

An unfinished RA '-' removes the grid with an explanation and remains exactly '-' in Photo tools. Repairing RA restores four outlines. At 320 × 568 the page client/scroll widths both remain 305. Malformed stored JSON refuses a grid and explains that Photo tools presents defaults before starting a new draft. The first implementation's generic retention wording was too broad for that fallback case; the final copy and component regression correct it. Signed altitude wording was also made explicit rather than using a negative angle followed by 'below'.

A custom RA 3 h/declination 0° grid straddles the horizon at this site/time, with centre altitude −1.3°. The 3D centre action is disabled, and the expanded 2D view visibly clips its cyan outline at the eastern rim. Final expanded 3D acceptance shows one canvas and its visible M31 marker at 390 px (dialog widths 388/388). ## 19 October 2026 — native 2025 light-pollution visual map

My places now offers an explicit Load light-pollution map action for David Lorenz’s 2025 artificial zenith-brightness model. The map uses locally bundled, lazily imported Leaflet 1.9.4, the provider’s year-specific 1024 px/zoom-offset −2 tile configuration and OpenStreetMap base tiles. Pan/zoom, keyboard navigation, selected-site marker, return-to-site, opacity and fifteen ratio-zone legend entries work independently of the planner date and personal Bortle rating. Map exploration does not select a new observing location. Numeric point sampling is not implemented.

The source page explicitly invites use of the maps with the distinction from Bortle classes. The direct model PNG response returned HTTP 200 and 1024² pixels; independent raw RGB inspection found exactly the fifteen palette colours used by the legend. A binary tile response was also checked for future sampler research, but no binary lookup code or copied raster is shipped. Detailed endpoint, terms, coverage and source limitations are recorded in LIGHT-POLLUTION-DATA.md. The library licence is retained in public/licenses/leaflet.txt and linked beside attribution. No provider analytics or CDN scripts are included.

Visible tile tracking is per layer and forgets unload/abort events. Late events cannot restore obsolete failures, and a loaded error placeholder cannot become valid data. Failed base/model tiles use a labelled hatch pattern with explicit guidance. Slow loading offers retry/close after a foreground-aware deadline. Retry retains opacity and returns focus to the map; Close returns focus to the load control. Renaming a site updates its accessible label without resetting its explored centre or opacity. Polar regions are masked; an out-of-coverage site uses a text fallback. Entered +180° is normalized only for rendering, retaining the saved coordinate identity.

Validation on pinned Node 22.16.0, with existing macOS dependencies: npm run verify passes all 690 tests, TypeScript, lint, production build and Cloudflare dry-run; 116 assets, no bindings. Five added regressions cover coverage/date-line inputs, pinned geometry, palette/ratio boundaries, partial health and retired/placeholder tile lifecycles. npm’s unrelated optional-package lockfile churn was removed; only the three added Leaflet/type package entries and direct dependency fields change.

Rendered acceptance used CUA in the in-app browser because the Browser plugin is not installed. The flow was My places → Load map → explore/adjust opacity → tile failure/retry → Close. The isolated compiled Worker was wrapped by a disposable visible QA fixture which routed new map-image requests to local synthetic PNGs. This avoids automated provider pan/zoom traffic and is not a claim of live raster-position accuracy. Separate normal HTTPS response/palette checks establish the actual external product evidence above.

## 19 October 2026 — saved-list focus recovery and generated PNG copies

Confirmed a pagination focus-loss bug in the compiled 98b8dad build: with 21 saved places and keyboard focus on Show next 1 saved places, a same-origin peer write reduced the list to 20. The controls unmounted and `document.activeElement` became BODY. The reproduction used 21 synthetic sites and a visible, eight-second armed peer-update fixture; no private/user records were used.

SavedListControls now retains a count message when pagination is unnecessary. A small React snapshot boundary captures focus ownership before a count change removes a control. It recovers only if that focused element was actually removed, the page still owns focus, and no other control has since taken it. A remaining pagination button is preferred; otherwise the count message receives focus. The message becomes visible with an outline while focused. Rendered review caught Tailwind’s clip-path still clipping the initially focused message; final CSS resets both clip and clip-path. Ordinary Show next / Show fewer handoffs remain unchanged and count-only recovery does not run for their shown-count changes.

Final compiled browser checks: 21 → 20 focuses visible “Showing all 20 saved places.” at 320 × 568 (305/305 document width, computed clip-path none); 21 → 0 focuses “No saved places to show.”; 41 → 40, with 40 rows shown and the final Show next button focused, recovers to Show fewer. Normal Show next focuses the first added row (QA site 21), and Show fewer returns to Show next. A peer update while Site name contained “Keep my unfinished name” left that input, its draft and focus intact. After focus moved to a separate parent-document input, the peer update did not focus anything in the app or take focus back. At 800 × 360 the document remained 785/785 px, with no relevant browser warning/error logs or framework overlay. This is rendered browser focus evidence, not a physical assistive-technology/screen-reader test.
