## 19 October 2026 — recover focus when a peer removes the saved row

A follow-up compiled-browser check found the corresponding row case in 43101c8: show all 21 saved places, focus QA site 21, then let an armed peer write keep only the first 20. The row disappeared and focus became BODY because the recovery boundary captured only pagination ownership. The boundary now also snapshots ownership inside its controlled list before DOM mutation. Row ownership is checked even when the count is unchanged, so replacing a focused row with another item can also recover. A surviving element is left alone; page-focus, modal visibility and newer-focus checks still apply.

The final compiled check recovers a removed QA site 21 to the visible “Showing all 20 saved places.” message at 320 × 568. Its clip-path is none, with 305/305 document width. A same-count last-row replacement keeps 21 rows and recovers to Show fewer. Ordinary Remove Peer replacement focuses its Undo button, and Undo focuses the existing Saved places heading rather than being intercepted by generic recovery. During another peer refresh, an unfinished “Retained during row refresh” Site name input retained both its text and focus. The observing instant stayed 2026-10-18T19:00:00.000Z. Short landscape stayed 785/785 px at 800 × 360. Meaningful content, no framework overlay and no relevant browser warning/error logs. These controlled saved-place flows are browser evidence, not blanket acceptance of every other saved-list workflow or physical assistive technology.

Full pinned-Node-22.16.0 npm run verify passes all 692 tests, TypeScript, lint, production build and Cloudflare dry-run (116 assets, no bindings). No extra mirrored unit test was added for the DOM lifecycle; before/after browser reproduction exercises the actual mutation/focus ordering. Release b2f5f71af036c0291a5098d7d714d209670be90e8d9b6263b73c1e93f842d21c reconstructs from 90 inputs with 330 resolved relative references, 106 client files, zero duplicate omissions, valid manifest/icons and matching offline/recovery bytes. Local dependencies were reused; hosted/physical-phone/final-origin gates remain pending. No push or deployment.

## 19 October 2026 — short landscape map height and resize-centre drift

Measured the map at 360 px tall inside an 800 × 360 viewport, leaving no vertical space beside the pan surface when it fills the view. The map now uses 55svh with bounded height on screens up to 500 px tall. Compiled measurements are 198 px at 800 × 360, while 320 × 568 portrait stays 300 px and 1100 × 800 desktop stays 360 px. Page widths remain 785/785, 305/305 and 1085/1085 respectively. This improves the room around the interactive map, not a physical touch/screen-reader acceptance claim.

The resize review exposed a separate real bug: ResizeObserver called Leaflet invalidateSize with pan:false, leaving the map’s geographic centre drifting when its width/height changed. A rotation cycle moved the displayed centre to 51.334°, −0.893° despite only a small eastward keyboard pan before resizing. The observer now requests a non-animated centre-preserving size update. In the final compiled check, the explored centre 51.509°, 0.316° at zoom 8 stayed identical through short landscape → portrait → desktop → short landscape. Zoom and keyboard panning worked; choosing the crosshair after that cycle selected the displayed coordinate site, focused the planner heading and retained 2026-10-18T19:00:00.000Z. No relevant browser warning/error logs or framework overlay. Map images were local synthetic fixtures; no automated provider pan/zoom traffic was used.

Full verification on pinned Node 22.16.0 passes all 692 tests, TypeScript, lint, production build and 116-asset/no-binding Cloudflare dry-run. An isolated manifest-only npm ci --dry-run --ignore-scripts --include=dev --include=optional --no-audit --no-fund exits 0 against the current package/lock files, validating lock consistency without installing packages or executing lifecycle scripts. It is not clean-install or Linux acceptance. The latest remote-main read still identifies b255260, with completed/success exact-head workflow 39166796103; newer local runtime changes still need a hosted run after the user’s batch push.

Final artifact release 0813a794e9dbf7aaf15777c930cb088f8a38bdf38b8006dcbd7ab710d298a671 reconstructs from 90 emitted/recovery inputs. All 330 literal relative references resolve; 106 client files, zero duplicate omissions, valid manifest/icons and matching offline/recovery bytes. No push or deployment.
